Martine Rose Is Balenciaga’s New Creative Director

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Martine Rose is Balenciaga's new creative director. The London-based designer will start on November 16, taking over the French-Spanish label from Pierpaolo Piccioli, whose departure was announced yesterday after three seasons at the house.

It's the first time Martine Rose has taken charge of a major fashion house, but it isn't her first Balenciaga gig. She worked as a menswear consultant under Demna from 2015 to 2018, helping shape the oversized proportions and left-field clothes that defined his early years at the brand. Now she's returning with control of the whole enterprise.

“It is a true privilege to be appointed creative director of Balenciaga, a house with an extraordinary heritage and a fearless creative spirit,” Rose said in a statement. “Balenciaga has been shaped by remarkable creative talents before me. I am committed to honoring this legacy and look forward to creating the next chapter in this rich and beautiful story.”

Rose founded her eponymous label in 2007 and has built it into one of the most influential brands in British fashion. Her collections pull from soccer and nightlife club culture, music, and the everyday wardrobes of London dwellers: suits get stretched out or squeezed in, tracksuits become tailoring, loafers turn into mules. It's menswear, on her terms.

Through her career, Rose has collaborated with Nike, Clarks, Supreme, Stüssy, and Tommy Hilfiger, and her clothes have been worn by everyone from Kendrick Lamar to Lewis Hamilton to Timothée Chalamet. But despite her idiosyncratic influence over what menswear looks like now, Rose has yet to have worked the top job at a house of Balenciaga's size. Her appointment makes her the first woman to lead the brand in its 109-year history. Her appointment makes her the first woman to lead the brand in its 109-year history.

“Martine Rose embodies this spirit with a fresh, fearless and deeply contemporary vision,” said Kering CEO Luca de Meo. “Her connection to the new generation and her instinctive understanding of culture bring a new dimension to the house.”

It's a big appointment, and one fashion people have wanted for a long time. Rumors that Rose had signed with Balenciaga have been swirling for weeks, but now it's official. The house hasn't yet said when her first collection will arrive, but whenever it does, we'll all be watching.

A version of this story originally appeared in British GQ.

Jalen Brunson’s Law & Order: SVU Debut Is Anything But Heinous

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

NBA star Jalen Brunson turned over an epic performance on the Nov. 5 episode of Law & Order: SVU, working alongside New York Knicks superfan Mariska Hargitay.

Just when it thought Jalen Brunson’s 2026 couldn’t get any better—having already clinched an NBA Championship title, a finals MVP award and an SNL hosting gig—he can also now add detective to his resume.

The New York Knicks point guard just assisted his good pal and Knicks superfan Mariska Hargitay with a guest appearance on the Nov. 5 episode of Law & Order: SVU.

In the scene, Hargitay’s Olivia Benson meets up with Brunson’s character in a restaurant, remarking, “Detective Madison. Look at you out of uniform, huh? Congratulations on the promotion.”

Brunson (a.k.a. Detective Madison) is seated at the counter dressed in nondescript clothing, save for the NYPD badge hanging around his neck.

“Thanks, Captain,” the detective, whose name is a cheeky hat-tip to the Knicks home stadium Madison Square Garden, responds. “Focused on my goals, gave it 110 percent. Things worked out.”

And in what is perhaps a veiled nod to the Knicks’ epic victory—during which Brunson was a key player who, in fact, did give it 110 percent—Benson responds with a small smile, “Yeah, they did.”

From there, the scene involves Det. Madison giving Benson the rundown on a former perp he got tangled with, calling him a “real pain in the ass.” Her interest is especially piqued, however, by his mention of a metal rod in the vehicle.

The info turns out to be a slam dunk, with the metal rod later confirmed as the weapon the episode’s victim had been attacked with, serving as the evidence the team needed to hopefully put the perp away for good.

And while Brunson’s cameo was nothing more than a fast break in the action of the episode, his presence still packed a punch.

“Short appearance but I loved it,” one fan wrote on Law & Order’s Instagram post teasing his appearance, while another added, “Jalen is so very talented!!!”

Brunson and Hargitay’s onscreen appearance was a perfect full-circle moment for fans, who have long loved seeing the Law & Order actress sitting courtside in MSG to cheer on Brunson and the team.

In fact, Brunson has gone so far as to even say Hargitay gets top billing on his personal Mt. Rushmore of celebrity Knicks fans.

“My number one, love her to death, and she knows it—Mariska Hargitay,” Brunson previously revealed to Richard Jefferson, who pointed out the Knicks player was blushing as he said it.

Thankfully, the feeling is very mutual, with Hargitay saying she feels “profoundly grateful” for Brunson’s leadership on the Knicks and how the team has “brought our city together.”

For more of the A-listers who would happily get a traveling violation just to sit courtside at a Knicks game, read on.

When it comes to Timothée Chalamet's fandoms, his love of the New York Knicks reigns supreme."My grandma was a huge Knicks fan, so that's what turned me onto them," the Marty Supreme star told 7PM in Brooklyn in 2025. "If you live in New York, you are a Knicks fan."

And if you've been keeping up, then you know Kylie Jenner has been joining Chalamet, whom she's dated since 2023, courtside to cheer on the Knicks.

You could never sever Ben Stiller's tie to the Knicks. In fact, the Severance director said that if he could choose between winning an Oscar or having the team take the championship title, he'd pick the latter."I really think, at the end of the day, if I knew that I had the choice, I would give it to the Knicks," he said on New Heights in 2025, "because I knew at least I had the choice in this weird, non-real situation."

Spike Lee is a longtime Knicks superfan and believes the 2025-2026 roster has what it takes to secure the trophy."I don’t care about San Antonio. OKC,” the filmmaker told the New York Post in May 2026. “We’re going to win.”

Nothing could rock Tracy Morgan's love for the Knicks. As the 30 Rock alum put it on The Rich Eisen Show in 2021, "I'm the biggest Knicks fan there is besides Spike."

Live from New York...it's Pete Davidson. The Saturday Night Live alum has often been seen sitting courtside.

Chris Rock's loyalty to the Knicks is no laughing matter as the comedian has attended many games over the years.

Larry David isn't going to curb his enthusiasm for the Knicks.

And neither is his costar JB Smoove."How it started vs How it ended," the actor wrote on Instagram in 2024. "It’ll always be Knicks for life."

Jordyn Woods' love for the Knicks extends on and off the court. The Woods by Jordyn founder is engaged to the team's center Karl-Anthony Towns.

While Cardi B has a song called "Bodak Yellow," she's all about that orange and blue. In fact, the rapper and former Patriots player Stefon Diggs officially debuted their romance at a 2025 game between the Knicks and the Boston Celtics.

Is Anne Hathaway wearing the  Chanel boots Knicks colors? Yeah, she is. The Devil Wears Prada star showed where her loyalties lie by donning an orange and blue pocket square to The Tonight Show in April 2026.

"I’m very chill," she told host Jimmy Fallon about her fandom. "I’m very loving and calm. But I’m also the type of fan that I considered wearing an OG jersey out here today."

Speaking of which, Fallon is also a big fan of the Knicks, with him even bringing his eldest daughter Franny along for the ride during the 2026 NBA playoffs.

You don't have to travel back to the future to see how much Michael J. Fox loves the Knicks. The actor has gone to many games with his wife Tracy Pollan.

Throughout the Knicks' ups and downs, Jon Stewart has continued to support the team, with The Daily Show host calling himself a "ride or die" fan.

A Knicks win is music to Maggie Rogers' ears as the singer has documented her love for the organization.

Fantasy Football Week 9 Fades & Busts: Bad times to continue for Panthers

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Welcome to the ninth of 17 weekly articles spotlighting the fantasy fades of the week! I am your host, Matt Okada, and I'll be bringing you half a dozen potential busts each and every Friday, from now until fantasy football championships.

As a note, just because a player earns a "fade" or "bust" designation doesn't automatically mean they should be benched — it depends on the rest of your roster or the options on your waiver wire. But you can expect them to fall short of expectations (when I get them right). I include a "What to Do" section for every player to help with that.

Without further ado, here is my list for Week 9 of the 2026 season.

Over the first two weeks of the season, Bryce Young was brilliant, throwing for three touchdowns and hitting 24+ fantasy points in both games. Then, he suffered a minor knee injury in Week 3, which he has played through with a brace, limiting his mobility in the pocket. He hasn't thrown three touchdowns in any of Carolina's six contests since, clocking in at just seven TDs and four interceptions over that span.

In the meantime, the Panthers' offensive line has been ravaged by injuries and, in combination, these factors have led to a severe drop-off in fantasy production for Young. He's been pressured on 50% of his dropbacks over the last two weeks and has logged a 35.4 passer rating and 20% success rate against that pressure. Largely as a result, he's averaged just 9.39 fantasy points per game in those two outings.

Now, he draws a matchup with a Broncos defense sporting the league's fourth-highest pressure rate (40.3%) and second-highest quick pressure rate (21.7%), per Next Gen Stats, both of which have increased in recent weeks. Young is likely to be in trouble early and often in this game and is staring down the barrel of another fantasy dud.

What to do ❓ Young is well outside QB1 range and is honestly closer to the back of QB2 range this week. I'd much prefer a streamer like Tyler Shough, Malik Willis, Kirk Cousins or even Michael Penix Jr.

Ollie Gordon II has been shockingly good for fantasy since taking over for De'Von Achane several weeks ago. Most of the fantasy community couldn't even decide whether he'd get the starting job, and he's proceeded to average 18.8 touches per game since the Week 3 takeover and score 13+ fantasy points in four of those five contests. However, I'm pumping the brakes for Week 9.

Coming off his first dud — 6.9 points against the tough Patriots run defense — Gordon has an even tougher matchup in the Lions. Detroit is a massive pass-funnel opponent, meaning that it encourages offenses to throw — allowing a league-high 298.7 yards per game on a league-high 41 attempts per game — rather than run (where it's bottom-10 in YPG and attempts per game allowed). As a result, it's allowing the fourth-fewest fantasy points per game to running backs. And unfortunately, Gordon has essentially zero role in the Miami passing attack. If he's lucky enough to see a goal-line carry or two, Gordon could score a touchdown and eke out a decent fantasy day, but it feels far more likely that he has another medium-volume, low-efficiency day and ends up in single-digit fantasy points once again.

What to do ❓ Gordon isn't an absolute must-sit, especially given the shallow nature of the position. But he's a low-end RB2 at best and I'd definitely lower expectations and avoid him in DFS.

Speaking of the Lions' run defense, they turned Jordan Mason into an absolute dud last week, with just 20 rushing yards on eight carries for 2.0 fantasy points. That brings his total since returning from injury in Week 7 to 88 yards on 23 carries (3.8 per carry) with zero catches and zero touchdowns. Fantasy managers who waited on his return are justifiably frustrated but might see a glimmer of hope in the "green" Bills matchup here in Week 9.

Buffalo has been a good matchup for running backs because its allowed the third-most receiving fantasy points per game, at 11.0, behind only the Dolphins and 49ers. In rushing fantasy points, its much closer to the middle of the pack, and Mason is only guaranteed to see a portion of that production with Aaron Jones Sr. still involved. The Vikings offense has been extremely lackluster all season and they're an underdog against the Bills in what figures to be a relatively low-scoring affair. None of this bodes well for the run-only half of Minnesota's RBBC, so Mason is better suited on the bench until a better opportunity arises ... if ever.

What to do ❓ Mason is well outside RB2 range and I wouldn't even consider him a promising FLEX. You're better off starting committee-backs like Zach Charbonnet or Blake Corum, as they should have better chances at scoring.

Remember everything I said about Bryce Young, the Panthers' O-line and the mess that pressure has created for this passing attack lately? All of that trickles down in a very discouraging way for early breakout candidate, Jalen Coker. He has just three catches on 10 targets all season when Young is pressured and just three catches for 45 yards during this particularly troubling downturn the past two weeks. He had a brilliant game against the Eagles three weeks ago, but Philly is currently the best matchup for wide receivers, so we have to take that 97-yard game with a grain of salt. And it makes sense that pressure and Coker don't get along: he's averaged 11.2 air yards per target and 20.5% of his targets have come on deep passes this season (nearly twice as high as the rate for Tetairoa McMillan).

Meanwhile, Denver has allowed the fifth-fewest fantasy points to wide receivers and the fourth-fewest yards per target on downfield throws (10+ air yards). Just as Young is a fade, Coker is a complementary fade for all the same reasons. I like his upside down the stretch if Young and the O-line can get healthy, but he's a very risky play in the meantime.

What to do ❓ Coker is outside WR2 range and while he's a playable FLEX in a pinch, I'd rather go with a lesser name in a better situation, like Keenan Allen or Stefon Diggs.

It seems like there's a bit of growing optimism about Jaylen Waddle in the fantasy community ... but I'm not entirely sure why. Sure, he caught a touchdown in Week 8 against the Chiefs ... just his second of the year, by the way. He also finished that game with three catches for 39 yards, marking his sixth-straight game with fewer than six catches and fewer than 50 receiving yards. His only truly impressive outing all season came back in Week 2 against the Jaguars — who have proven to be a major pass-funnel defense — when he saw eight catches for 138 yards on 10 targets. But Waddle had 138 receiving yards in his other six games combined entering Week 8 and is averaging just 39.4 per game on the season, even when including that aberration of an outing against Jacksonville.

He's averaging the fourth-fewest yards per target (6.2) among all wideouts with 50+ targets and doesn't have a game over 14 fantasy points in the last six weeks. All told, his floor and ceiling are concerningly low and I see little reason for optimism against the Panthers — even with injuries to their starting cornerbacks — given how spotty and inefficient Waddle's usage has been.

What to do ❓ Waddle falls a couple spots behind Coker in my rankings, so we can apply the same logic for him in Week 9 — WR3 at best, iffy FLEX play, would rather go with Allen or Diggs.

This pains me, as I am a huge Dalton Kincaid fan, and there are a lot of reasons to believe in him rest-of-season — like his position-leading 2.7 yards per route run and his robust-for-a-tight-end air yards share (19.7%). But the Minnesota Vikings are terrifying. They've allowed a league-low 5.5 fantasy points per game to tight ends and have held every single TE they've faced to single-digit fantasy points. Tucker Kraft set the current high-water mark at just 8.0 points, while Sam LaPorta (5.0), Juwan Johnson (3.6), Colston Loveland (0.8) and others have been complete duds in this matchup.

On average, they've surrendered just 3.1 catches for 39.0 yards per game to the position, and have yet to allow a touchdown to a tight end all season. Sitting Kincaid won't be easy, especially given the shallow nature of the position, but it might be necessary given the heavily reduced ceiling against Minnesota.

What to do ❓ Try to find a streamer in redraft if you can, a la T.J. Hockenson, Kyle Pitts Sr., or even Brenton Strange, rather than playing Kincaid against this defense. And stay away in DFS.

Fox bought Roku

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Fox now has control of Roku’s streaming platform, which reaches more than 100 million US households.

Fox now has control of Roku’s streaming platform, which reaches more than 100 million US households.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Fox has officially closed its $22 billion acquisition of Roku, the companies announced on Friday. With Roku under its umbrella, Fox now controls a major streaming platform that reaches more than 100 million households in the US.

First announced in June, the deal will allow Fox to bring Fox Sports, news content, and local stations to Roku. “I would expect that we can grow our viewership in the US with a combination of Roku technology, Roku’s platform, the tremendous content that’s on The Roku Channel, and the content that Fox brings to it as well,” Fox CEO Lachlan Murdoch said at the time. Along with Roku, Fox also operates its own Fox One streaming service and the free streamer Tubi.

Though the deal might change what Roku showcases on its platform, it doesn’t look like it will affect the open nature of its streaming ecosystem. “We started Roku more than two decades ago to give people a better way to watch TV, and that mission doesn’t change today. What does change is our reach and resources,” Roku founder Anthony Wood said. “Fox has a deep respect for Roku’s culture and wholly shares our commitment to staying open to every content partner.” Wood now serves on Fox’s board of directors.

NFL Week 9 underrated games: An international game, two AFC vs. NFC showdowns to add to your watch list

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Week 9 of 2026 is an exciting one. The trade deadline is right around the corner, and teams have until Nov. 10 at 4 p.m. ET to sell or add to their team.

There have already been moves made, and rumors are swirling about multiple players across the league. This will be a big week for teams to watch players they're interested in to determine if they want to make a move.

There are some obvious big games this week, but there are also some underrated games you should be tuning into. Here is a look at three games that should be on your radar:

Sunday, Nov. 8 at 9:30 a.m. ET on NFL Network

Who doesn't love to wake up and immediately watch football? The international game this week is in Spain, which already adds an exciting extra element to the matchup.

These two teams, specifically their quarterbacks, are coming off wildly different games. Last week, Falcons QB Michael Penix had a career-high four interceptions in their 16-0 loss to the Buccaneers. Bengals QB Joe Burrow threw five touchdown passes and ran in the overtime game-winner in their 43-36 battle against the Titans.

The matchup I'm interested in is the Falcons' defense against the Bengals' passing game, specifically wide receivers Tee Higgins and Ja'Marr Chase. Higgins is second in yards per game (103.7), while Chase is 16th in the league (70.3), making them a duo very difficult for secondaries to stop. The Bengals lead the NFL in passing yards per game (288.4), and the Falcons defense is in the middle of the pack in passing yards allowed (223.4).

The Bengals come in at 5-2, while the Falcons are 3-5 but have shown real flashes of strong play. Coming off two vastly different games, plus international travel, this game could surprise people.

Sunday, Nov. 8 at 1 p.m. ET on CBS, streaming on Paramount+

This one isn't a divisional, or even conference, matchup, so it will go under the radar more than some of the direct intraconference showdowns, but I think it will be one of the best games of the week.

The Broncos are riding high after a huge divisional win over the Chiefs, in a game that came down to the final play. They now lead the AFC West and are looking to keep that status and extend their four-game win streak.

In their way are the Panthers, who should come out aggressive, looking to snap a two-game losing streak. Despite being 3-4, the Panthers lead the NFC South in a battle of rebuilding teams, with up-and-down performances.

Carolina is ninth in yards per game (363.7), while the Broncos defense has allowed the eighth-most yards per game (355.3), creating an interesting matchup. Another intriguing element of the game goes back to the trade deadline. The Broncos have been tied heavily to running back Jonathan Taylor, who is a top trade candidate.

Sunday, Nov. 8 at 8:20 p.m. ET on NBC (Stream: fubo, try for free)

Both teams are coming off wins, but in very different fashions. The Bucs defeated the Falcons 16-0 without completing a single pass. The run game thrived in the rainy game, with Bucky Irving finishing with 170 yards and Kenneth Gainwell rushing for 140 yards.

The Bears won their last three, including a big win in primetime last week against the defending Super Bowl champion Seahawks. Chicago is second in the NFC North in a tight race with the Vikings, who are 6-1.

A battle between Bucs QB Baker Mayfield and Bears QB Caleb Williams has big potential, and with both teams in tight divisional races and sitting in second place in their respective divisions, both teams have playoff aspirations. Chicago is the better team, more likely to go further in the postseason, but with Tampa settling back in with Mayfield's return, they could improve down the stretch.

Another player to watch during the game is defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons, who will be playing his second game with the Bears after a blockbuster trade with the Titans.

Iraola: We must sacrifice control for creativity to fix scoring woes

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola calls on Liverpool to sacrifice control of games in order to score more after struggles in front of goal; watch Crystal Palace vs Liverpool live on Sky Sports Main Event from 12.30pm on Sunday kick-off 2pm

Football Journalist @Ronnabe

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

Liverpool must sacrifice control in games in order to fix their creativity problems, Andoni Iraola has demanded ahead of their weekend trip to Crystal Palace, live on Sky Sports.

The Reds have created the second-fewest big chances in the Premier League this season and scored only four goals in five home games, their joint-fewest at this stage of a season in their history.

They set an unwanted record against Arsenal last weekend, failing to register a shot on target in a home Premier League game for the first time in more than 440 games since Opta records began in 2003.

Iraola arrived at Anfield last summer with a reputation for front-foot, direct football but has had difficulties imposing his philosophy on his new players in the first three months of the season, and now wants them to take more risks to turn their domination into clear-cut opportunities.

He said: "Obviously, we want to score more and concede fewer. It's a matter of controlling the games but also sometimes, against Fenerbahce we were controlling the game too much.

"In possession we can have good positions, but sometimes we have to throw numbers inside the box, arrive with more players.

"It will take a little bit of control away, but it will give us more threat when we arrive. Especially against Arsenal, the way we attacked the box had to be better.

"We are one of the teams which arrives the most in the final third. In terms of volume of shots and crosses, we are very high - but if you analyse the quality level of chance, it is low.

"When we start losing games, attacking against a lot of positions, it's not going to be the quality of the chances.

"The number of times we get to the box, we have to arrive better. Even if that means losing some control, we have to get into the six-yard box more. It will give us more clear shots."

Liverpool's 1.05xG in Sunday's 1-0 defeat to Arsenal would have been significantly boosted had they been awarded a clear penalty for a shirt pull by Jurrien Timber on Alexander Isak.

Iraola admitted after the game he felt a spot kick would have been awarded had Isak gone to ground, and after being praised by the officials in Wednesday's Champions League defeat at Fenerbahce feels his side can be "too honest".

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"We are an honest group. Too honest, sometimes," he said. "The referee congratulated us after the game on Wednesday, and I don't know if I liked that.

"It has to be genuine. You cannot fake things. When we complain, it has to be a genuine complaint.

"I feel we are willing, obviously, we're a team that wants to press and be intense. But the balance there is a big difference."

Isak has scored six of Liverpool's 11 Premier League goals this season after struggling with injury and form in his first year at Anfield.

Despite the Reds' wider goalscoring problems he sits second only to Erling Haaland in the race for the Golden Boot, leaving Iraola full of praise for the forward who has already doubled his league tally from last season.

"Alex is a top goal scorer," he said. "If we can put him more in the position to score he will deliver, that is not a guarantee but is the closest thing to a guarantee.

"Like every player, he can add more things to his game but we want to use [more] the things that he has. He is very good at attacking the space, attacking the channels, coming to receive the ball is very clean, also.

"We are trying to use Isak in the best way."

"Liverpool's total of 140 shots this season is the fifth-highest among Premier League sides. But they rank much lower for both goals scored and expected goals, in ninth and 11th respectively.

"Their shot locations help to explain the disparity. Closer-range shots typically have a higher probability of going in. Liverpool, though, have taken a large chunk of their total from long distance.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

"The Reds have the fourth-highest percentage of shots from outside the box in the division at 39 per cent, behind only Manchester United, Hull and Newcastle, all of whom sit in the bottom half of the Premier League table nine games into the season.

"By contrast, Liverpool's proportion of shots from inside the six-yard box is the sixth-lowest in the division, at eight per cent.

"It is worth noting the two sides with the highest percentages of shots from inside the six-yard box are leaders Arsenal and second-placed Manchester City on 17 per cent and 13 per cent respectively."

Arsenal: Mikel Arteta sends France clear William Saliba message before squad selection

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Mikel Arteta insists that Arsenal would have no issue with William Saliba being called up to the France squad.

Saliba has not played a single minute of football for either club or country so far this season, having been on the sidelines with a back injury since he was forced off in the World Cup semi-final defeat by Spain in July.

The centre-back has stepped up his recovery in recent weeks and on Wednesday made his first return to Arsenal’s matchday squad.

Saliba was an unused substitute against Slavia Prague in the Champions League and it remains to be seen whether he is involved against Hull in the Premier League on Saturday.

There is a chance that Saliba’s first minutes this season come with France rather than Arsenal, with the defender in contention to be named in Zinedine Zidane’s latest Les Bleus squad.

Arsenal have been in discussions with the French medical staff over Saliba’s situation.

“We’ve been in very close contact,” Arteta said on Friday. “They’re announcing [their squad] at 5pm this evening.

“They have all the information, now it’s about them, about Zinedine in this case to make the call. We will accept whatever it is.”

Arteta also suggested that Saliba joining up with France could be a positive for Arsenal, as the club look to build his fitness.

France travel to take on Italy next Thursday before hosting Turkey three days later in their final Nations League matches.

Asked whether he had concerns about Saliba being named in the French squad, Arteta said: “It’s something that is between them more.

“William needs minutes as well. I know how much he loves it, to be in and around the national team.

“It’s a new manager [Zidane], a really exciting one as well. Whatever is decided, it’s fine for us.”

Elche vs Real Betis - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Matt Law | 06 Nov 2026 14:48, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 14:51

Real Betis will aim to make it successive wins in Spain's top flight when they continue their campaign against Elche at Estadio Martinez Valero on Sunday afternoon.

The visitors posted a 2-1 victory over Malaga in their last league match, while Elche will enter the contest off the back of a 1-0 home defeat to Valencia.

Elche boast a record of two wins, four draws and five defeats from their 11 league matches this season, with 10 points leaving them in 17th spot in the La Liga table.

The Green-Striped Ones were beaten 1-0 by Valencia in their last match, but that proved to be their first loss in five games in all competitions.

Martin Anselmi's side have actually won two of their last three matches, including a 3-1 victory over Pinatar in the Copa del Rey at the end of October.

Elche have struggled in their recent meetings with Real Betis, losing four of the last five fixtures between the two sides, but the corresponding game last season finished 1-1.

The Green-Striped Ones have not managed to overcome Real Betis since April 2022, while their last home success over the Seville outfit came back in November 2009.

Real Betis lead the overall head-to-head record between the two sides, having posted 24 wins to Elche's 16, while 17 of their 57 games have finished all square.

Manuel Pellegrini's side were in Champions League action during the week, suffering a 2-1 defeat to Borussia Dortmund in the league stage of the competition.

The Seville outfit were 2-1 winners over Malaga last time out, with that success making their record seven wins, two draws and two defeats from their 11 league matches this season, which has left them fourth in the table on 23 points.

Real Betis are only four points off third-placed Real Madrid and are, as expected, firmly in the Champions League picture at this stage of the La Liga campaign.

El Glorioso have a record of two wins, two draws and one defeat from their five away league matches this season, scoring six times and conceding seven.

Elche will be without the services of Marc Aguado and Thomas Lemar for this weekend's match through injury, but the home side are otherwise in strong form.

Yago Santiago and Gonzalo Villar had both been doubts for this contest, but the pair are available for selection, while Pedro Bigas should also again be in the squad.

Lemar's position in the starting side is likely to be taken by Facundo Buonanotte, while there will be a spot through the middle for Fer Nino, who has netted six times in La Liga this season.

As for Real Betis, Cucho Hernandez remains on the sidelines and is unlikely to be back in the fold for the Seville outfit until the end of November.

Facundo Bernal is still suspended, but Junior Firpo and Alvaro Fidalgo are both expected to be back in the squad after recent absences.

Troy Parrott has not scored for Real Betis since the start of September, but the 24-year-old is expected to continue through the middle for Pellegrini's team.

Dituro; Sangare, Chust, Redondo, Revivo; Villar, Morcillo; Cepeda, Buonanotte, Valera; Nino

Valles; Bellerin, Bartra, Natan, Garcia; Roca, Fornals; Antony, Isco, Ezzalzouli; Parrott

Elche have been in solid form of late, and this is not a straightforward match for Real Betis, but we are backing the Seville outfit to secure a valuable three points on Sunday.

The Pacers are getting a sobering reminder of how fragile NBA success can be

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It is tempting to hand-waive everything that has happened to the Indiana Pacers in the last year and a half as simply the work of some vengeful spirit, unfairly punishing an innocent fanbase who never did anything to deserve this kind of sustained injury catastrophe and lottery luck that would sink any franchise, in Indiana or on the Moon. And hand-waive we shall. But waive enough hands, and the cumulative effect of this bad luck will start slipping through our fingers unnoticed, like grains of sand piling up into a castle the size of the Gainbridge Fieldhouse, threatening to crush us all if we don't make infrastructure improvements.

I was out on the Pacers coming into the year, though I never found the time to publish that take anywhere preseason with the NFL kicking off and the MLB headed for the World Series and then World War III. — you'll just have to trust my editors that I've been talking about it. My concern lay on the fact that a 50-32 Pacers team in 2024-25 had ridden one of the most absurd stretches of clutch shot-making ever to Game 7 of the NBA Finals, lost Tyrese Haliburton to a torn Achilles in one of the most heart-wrenching injuries in NBA history, lost said Game 7, lost Myles Turner to some Bucks stupidity and then went 19-63 (yes, that is nineteen and sixty-three) the following year with a roster I thought … was not that bad.

Every Pacers take I heard last year from people in NBA media more connected than I started and ended with the same two words: "gap year." This 19-63 record was, of course, intentional, as the team tanked for a top draft pick to play when Haliburton was back the following season. But then they made a splashy move, wagering that draft pick on a bold 50-50 with the Clippers for Ivica Zubac, protecting that pick in a specific way that meant if it fell out of the top four, they would lose it. Well … it did, and their 19-63 record netted them nothing in one of the most stacked top fours we've ever seen. Once again: heartbreaking.

Then the actual 2026 season rolled around, and their roster looked solid; at the very least, it would be competitive, if not actually a Finals contender in my mind. And it was going reasonably well for about seven minutes until Haliburton suffered a hamstring injury (like are you serious right now) and the rest of the team got injured along with him. Now 3-6, last week's lineups would make you think they were tanking again.

Their actual players will slowly make it back in time, but the question must officially be asked (no, not "is this team injury prone?" that's a stupid question): do the Pacers have the roster to cope with even one injury, and can this group survive a wide-open but packed Eastern Conference, one with aspiring playoff teams popping out of drawers? Betting markets aren't really sure what to think, with Indiana holding the ninth-best odds in the East at 18-1 on FanDuel. They're squarely out of the top group of Boston, New York and Philadelphia, and not even really in the second group of Toronto, Detroit and Cleveland either.

These are trying realities, and beg one concerning question: even fully healthy, are the Pacers a contender in the first place?

The argument for Indiana coming into the year was basically that Tyrese Haliburton was one of the five most important players in the league, if not actually one of the five best. Sure, there was tanking last year, but many of Indiana's NBA Finals roster played a decent number of games in 2025-26 and they still couldn't get to 20 wins. Unless this was the single most coordinated, organizationally coherent tanking job in history, with unprecedented buy-in from … Pascal Siakam, the only explanation for the 19-63 record was that Haliburton was basically Michael Jordan-level important for Indiana.

And he definitely was the linchpin of everything they did. But none of the advanced stats suggested that the team would literally evaporate without Haliburton. He's not some kind of Net Rating god, and I repeatedly found myself confused as to how the Pacers were losing every game despite a noticeable talent advantage on other teams in their tanking bracket.

That's a long way to say that I don't think that roster was actually that good, and the Finals run was more of an out-of-control party of unrepeatable clutch ridiculousness than it was the actual quality of Indiana basketball. If you accept that (you may not, but just … accept it for now so the grammar of the next clause works), your opinion of this Pacers team in 2026 comes down to how you feel about their upgrades — Kelly Oubre Jr. is a fine depth wing, but Ivica Zubac is the money question. Is he actually better than Myles Turner was in 2024?

With Tyrese Haliburton, I think he can be. Haliburton is a one-of-one elite table-setter for big men, and Zubac needs him on the floor to elevate his offensive game beyond simple, prodding interior hoops. Sidebar: I saw a Pacers fan complaining on Reddit that he doesn't feel like Zubac even looks to shoot the three-pointer even when he's wide open, which I find hysterical since he has made one singular 3-pointer in his 11-year career. But hey, he's open.

So far, though, Zubac has been uninspiring on the Pacers, and he hasn't been a particularly special defender in a few years like Turner was in his prime (yes, that is me declaring Myles Turner's prime over, have you watched a Bucks game recently?). Zubac must be a walking double-double for the Pacers to raise their ceiling.

Without Haliburton, this is all completely moot. They're in survival mode right now, needing to stack wins wherever they can find them until they can bring back a competent roster nightly. But even with Haliburton, I think this roster might just be bringing a squeaky plastic hammer to a knife fight. Like, you might be able to block some slashes and win hand-to-hand combat, and nobody in the eastern conference has a gun … but we have a squeaky plastic hammer. And right now, it has a hole in it.

Is Jobe Bellingham England's next big thing who shares his older brother's 'X-Factor' - or a 'problem child' whose dad blows up at his bosses? Inside the rise of the 21-year-old after first call-up

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 09:16 EDT, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 10:24 EDT, 6 November 2026

For years, Jobe Bellingham has been in the shadows of his older brother Jude.

Now, for the first time since they were children, the duo are poised to perform on the same stage. Only this time it won't be under the lampposts of Stourbridge, but the floodlights at Wembley.

Jobe, 21, has been called up to England's senior squad for the first time and is often described in similar terms to his elder sibling: relentlessly ambitious, focused, and a quick learner who is wise beyond his years.

'You have to have an X-Factor and Jobe does,' said Regis Le Bris, his old manager at Sunderland.

That X-Factor appears to run in the Bellingham blood. Earlier this week Jude was unveiled as the new underwear heart-throb for Calvin Klein, channelling his inner David Beckham in a smouldering photoshoot. No pressure, Jobe.

Their main difference on the pitch is that Jobe, while versatile, tends to occupy a deeper-lying role in midfield – and that actually increases the chances of Thomas Tuchel fielding them together. There is every chance that, against Croatia or Spain next week, we will see just the fourth pair of brothers to feature for England since the Second World War.

Jude and Jobe Bellingham's dream of playing for England together may finally come true

The duo grew up playing together and both joined the Birmingham City academy (Instagram: judebellingham)

The duo visited the Spanish Grand Prix in September with matching shirts and haircuts

They share a close relationship and in September were VIP guests of McLaren for the Spanish Grand Prix, posing for photographs in matching blue shirts and identical haircuts, a mischievous double act.

If you were a few pints deep in the Formula One hospitality area, you'd be forgiven for thinking they were the same bloke – especially when their footballing trajectories are so similar.

Jobe began his career at Birmingham City, like Jude, before moving to Sunderland and now Borussia Dortmund, where his brother also played. Jobe, who is two years younger than his superstar brother, is now in his second full season with the Westphalia giants.

He has previously admitted his trepidation at following in Jude's footsteps, once telling the German club's in-house media channel: 'I was biased against joining Borussia Dortmund for a very long time.

'I had anxieties, fears, and you want to be your own man. But I don't think I should let those stop me from making the right decision.'

The comparisons are inevitable, though, when you share the same name and your potential also happens to be through the ceiling. It is a theme Jobe will simply have to contend with wherever he roams.

In Dortmund's 2-2 draw with Bayern Munich on Saturday, the resemblance was striking as he delivered a standout performance – even having the ball in the back of the net, though his goal was ruled out for a Carney Chukwuemeka foul.

At one stage, Bellingham backheeled the ball over Aleksandar Pavlovic, won the ball back off Dayot Upamecano, drifted past Pavlovic with a skill that left him on one knee, and drove about 30 yards towards goal before firing a shot just over the bar.

They would be just the fourth pair of brother to play for England since the Second World War

Jude and Jobe are pictured together at a Louis Vuitton Paris Fashion Week show in 2023

In that ten-second flash, he displayed the sort of tenacity, ingenuity, and swagger which we have come to associate with Jude.

While not as brazen on the surface as Jude, Jobe perhaps gets some of his confidence from their father Mark.

The brothers were introduced to football by their dad, a retired police sergeant who also scored 700 goals in non-league.

Raising his sons in the West Midlands alongside their mother Denise, Mark gave them tips on how to improve and how to deal with the pressures of football. Jude even describes his father as his first footballing hero.

The crackerjack duo have always competed to see who can be the most precocious. Jude became Birmingham City's youngest-ever player when he debuted for them in 2019. When Jobe made his bow for them in 2022, he did so just 69 days older.

He earned his move to Sunderland after just 26 games and was a hit on Wearside. In his second campaign, he won the Championship Young Player of the Season award and helped them to promotion via the play-offs.

Manager Le Bris was breathless in his praise. 'There are many [qualities], there is a long list,' he said. 'Probably his ambition is one of the first ideas I have when I think about Jobe.

'He is very ambitious but this ambition is not naive. It is connected with what he is doing every day in training and every game on the pitch.

Jude pictured with his brother Jobe, father Mark, and mother Denise upon signing for Borussia Dortmund in 2020

'He has many good qualities as a footballer. He is very powerful and he understands the game very well. He is really interested in the game and, because of that, he is able to improve with experiences. He can learn very quickly.

'He is older than his age. He has more maturity. He is like an exception in the sport. It is not easy to play at the level he is doing so at his age, but he is doing so well.'

In the summer of 2025, Dortmund got on the blower. They were willing to pay £31million – more than they had for Jude – and put him straight in the squad for the Club World Cup. Dortmund's UK scout is Daniel Dodds, the brother of Mike, who had worked with Jobe in Birmingham's academy and at Sunderland.

'I think he can go to the very top,' Mike told the Northern Echo in 2023. 'Both brothers have a very similar mindset in terms of their relentlessness in their pursuit of where they want to get to.'

Despite the extravagant projections, Jobe's time in Germany's industrial belt has not been entirely smooth. Often he has been left to simmer on the bench, unable to nail down a regular starting spot.

In his first Klassiker experience, he came on only to lose the ball and have Michael Olise's shot deflect in off him. One report described him as a man who looked 'completely stripped of confidence'.

On the opening day of last season against St Pauli, he was hauled off at the break, leading to his father going ballistic and allegedly confronting sporting director Sebastian Kehl. That put Jobe in an awkward spot. He was also sent off for a professional foul against Freiburg.

Amid his struggles to adapt to the Bundesliga, he has copped criticism off the ferocious pundit Dietmar Hamann, who blasted him as a 'problem child' in a column for Sky Sport. 'Young, expensive and, so far, not even half as good as Jude Bellingham,' seethed the former Liverpool midfielder.

Jobe has become a more regular starter since the turn of the year and, in any case, has always been highly thought of in English football circles.

Jobe helped Sunderland to promotion from the Championship - manager Regis Le Bris said he had the 'X-factor'

Lee Carsley made Jobe his captain for the Under-21s and waxed lyrical about him: 'I think he will be an all-round box-to-box player. He can be creative, score goals. I still feel he can score more goals.

'His shooting is excellent, especially in training. He can link and receive in tight areas.'

Carsley added: 'He’s a good example to the rest of the group, the way he conducts himself on and off the pitch, the way he prepares, the influence he has on the rest of the team.

'He’s a player who can grow into that role. We just assume that players understand leadership and how that works. It will be good to add another string to his bow and keep improving his game.'

After his U21s debut against Spain in Cadiz, he was ambushed by people who wanted signatures... on their Real Madrid shirts. It was opportune and distasteful, but reflected the bigger picture: the name Bellingham is associated with Jude.

Now Jobe's moment has come. Tuchel picked him partly because of Carsley jabbering on with praise in his ear over recent months. The noise around him has become unavoidable and, with Alex Scott injured and Myles Lewis-Skelly suspended for the first game, the Three Lions are light in midfield.

'To play with my brother for England that would be the biggest dream of my life. Nothing would even get close to that,' Jude said in 2024. That may soon be a reality.

Inside Mohamed Salah's new life after his Liverpool fall-out: LEWIS STEELE visits Turkey's 30th biggest city, where the Egyptian King hikes, drinks salted yoghurt - and still gets hero worship

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 07:03 EDT, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 10:12 EDT, 6 November 2026

It started just minutes after touchdown in Turkey.

In a taxi journey across Istanbul's intercontinental bridge to Fenerbahce's stadium, the driver took a break from lighting up a cigarette or shouting profanities at other road users. He turned, looked me in the eye, and asked: 'You know Mohamed Salah?'

Two and a half hours after fighting through mind-blowing traffic (think your gridlocked motorway at 5pm multiplied by 100), on arrival at Liverpool's press conference, one gentleman did not even exchange pleasantries. He shoved a selfie stick in my face and asked about Salah.

And these two encounters were 650 miles away from where the Egyptian player now plies his trade. This fandom was only a starter when compared to the main course of Salahmania that greeted us upon arrival in the port city of Trabzon, to the east of Turkey on the coast of the Black Sea.

Here, his face and name were everywhere you looked. 'You come for Salah?' enquired the hotel receptionist. Upon hearing that was indeed the purpose of the visit, he upgraded the room to a superior suite overlooking the ocean. Result.

The streets of this city, which feels a world apart from the bustling metropolis of Istanbul, never mind Liverpool, are packed with antique dealers, and markets selling two-foot-long fish for less than £1 and the local delicacy of hamsi (anchovy to you and me).

Mohamed Salah can relax in Trabzon and has taken multiple hiking trips in the highlands (Source: Instagram/Mohamed Salah)

Fans have now taken to posing at the same spots Salah appeared at - such is their hero worship

Everywhere you turned, there was a nod to Salah. His name was heard in conversations as the melodic call to prayer rang out from the nearby Hagia Sophia mosque, his picture seemed to be hung from every building and his name was on the back of every shirt being sported in town.

Shops were selling mugs, scarves, plates, masks and even cigarette lighters shaped like Salah.

At the futuristic waterfront stadium known as Papara Park, Salahmania continued - with punters putting on impromptu barbecues and DJ sets from beside their cars similar to tailgates in American sports. Everyone in this city was dancing to the beat of the Egyptian King.

Fans, many in Trabzonspor-coloured jester hats with bells on, glowed about Salah. Most do not speak a word of English in this city not far from the Armenian and Georgian border. But those who could summed up the mood.

'We love him, he is going to make us champions,' said one.

Another told us: 'People across the country now support Trabzonspor and people come from other countries, people want us to win just because of Mohamed Salah.'

Trabzonspor supporters told Daily Mail Sport: 'We love him, he is going to make us champions'

Everywhere you turned there was a nod to Salah in his new club's kit

There were plenty of children in the crowd, and more women than expected. Some had come from afar to see him, others could not believe that Salah had chosen their team and hometown.

So, why Trabzon? Why did Salah choose this project in Turkey's 30th biggest city? Why not one of the three Istanbul giants – Fenerbahce, Galatasaray or Besiktas – who have won 28, 27 and 21 Turkish titles respectively, compared to Trabzonspor's seven, including only one since 1984.

The answers are obvious, according to sources. Salah, now 34, wanted a place where he could enjoy his football without the intense scrutiny. Every word and facial expression would generate days of news coverage in Liverpool or Istanbul or another one of Europe's giant cities.

On the outskirts of Trabzon, Salah can relax, hiking up to a group of boulders in Yayla. Even there, he's worshipped, though. Trabzonspor fans flock there to pose for photographs on the same rock where he sat for a photo posted on his Instagram.

Salah's family are settled, he is becoming obsessed with chess and he has started drinking the Turkish salted-yoghurt drink ayran each day.

On the pitch, there is a belief Trabzonspor can beat the big three and end Galatasaray's four-year grip on the title.

The team also stars former Liverpool midfielder Fabinho and ex-Manchester United goalkeeper Andre Onana, while Jerome Boateng, once a stalwart at Bayern Munich, is assistant manager. Boss Thomas Reis was poached from Ludogorets and has lost just once since his arrival.

Salah is settled with his family and faces less scrutiny than in Istanbul or Liverpool (Source: Instagram/Mohamed Salah)

Salah has scored 10 goals and assisted five more in his 15 games in Turkey so far

The highlight so far came on September 19, when Salah scored a hat-trick in a 4-0 thumping of Galatasaray. Trabzonspor have won seven league games in a row and will aim to go deep in the Conference League despite Thursday's undeserved 1-0 defeat to Freiburg.

Salah has scored 10 goals and assisted in five more in his 15 games in Turkey.

'Everyone wants to work with a star the size of Mohamed Salah,' striker Paul Onuachu, once of Southampton, told us after that game. 'He is a top guy and a world star.

'He adds so much to us and helps all of us, thanks to his experience and quality, and he contributes positively to the development of our team.

'I am grateful for him. It is a blessing to play with him.'

Salah did not have the rub of the green in the clash with the German side, who were Europa League finalists last year. But a trip to Papara Park underlined an answer to those wondering just why he picked Trabzon and not any of his other mega-money offers.

A man wearing Trabzonspor's claret and light blue colours shows off his support for Salah

A trip to Papara Park underlined an answer to those wondering just why he picked Trabzon

He had approaches from America, and the Saudi Arabian federation tried to sign him right until the day he jetted into Trabzon - where he was greeted by 35,000 fans with fireworks and flares. Besiktas held advanced talks with Salah's agent Ramy Abbas.

But Trabzonspor won the race and Salah is playing with a smile again, even though that face pulled a bit of a grimace when he spotted a familiar face or two from the English press pack waiting for him after the game.

His laugh faded into an expression that said: 'I thought I got rid of you lot pestering me!'

That face confirmed his reason for joining Trabzonspor: Salah is out of the spotlight – certainly while still in with a chance to win trophies and maintain a good level of football until next summer's Africa Cup of Nations.

After a public fall-out with Liverpool that saw the final year of his contract ripped up and him accuse the Anfield club of 'throwing him under the bus', it feels like Salah is feeling at home and happy again.

It's not difficult to turn down the multiple opportunities to buy Salah-branded magnets or shirts or teddies or socks or lighters as we bid farewell to Trabzon, but it is hard to escape the conclusion that the feeling of love is mutual in this football-mad city.

Michael Carrick accepts he won't be given time to emulate Sir Alex Ferguson as Unai Emery confirms Aston Villa contract talks in blow to Man United: Premier League LIVE

It's the final Premier League gameweek before the international break, and managers up and down the country will be speaking to the press.

Daily Mail Sport will bring you all the key news here...

Mikel Arteta said Max Dowman is raring to impress with the England Under 21s after being called up for the first time.

The 16-year-old Arsenal attacker has been selected by head coach Lee Carsley for the November friendlies against Portugal and France, with Dowman having previously represented England up to Under 19 level.

Should he feature in one of the matches, the teenager would become the youngest player to represent England's Under 21s, surpassing the record set by Jude Bellingham.

Mikel Arteta said Max Dowman is raring to impress with the England Under 21s next week

Arteta said: 'It's great news, I think he's very excited about that and now he has another contribution to do with the national team.

'Unfortunately, he cannot play for us in the next few games, but he will have the chance to play with the national team, which is great.'

Michael Carrick believes it's possible to emulate Sir Alex Ferguson's success at Manchester United, but accepts he won't be given as much time to turn the club's fortunes around as his former boss.

On the 40th anniversary of Ferguson replacing Ron Atkinson in 1986 with United one place above the relegation zone, Carrick's team are in an identical position having equalled Atkinson's club record worst start of one win from the first nine games.

A rousing win over Roma in the Champions League on Tuesday has lifted the mood around Old Trafford and eased some of the pressure on Carrick ahead of Sunday's game at home to Aston Villa.

Ferguson survived a barren three-and-a-half years in charge before winning the FA Cup in 1990. It was the first over 38 trophies over more than quarter of a century, and Carrick was asked if it would be impossible to match that.

'Well, it's not impossible,' said Carrick. 'Anything's possible. You've got to build, you know. You've got to be realistic. You've got to put things in perspective as well. The recent history that we've had and where we want to get to, it's not going to happen in a blink of an eye. It's not going to happen overnight. Success does take time, and we don't like having to spend time. But it does take a period of time to build.'

Asked if he will be given the same amount of time as Ferguson after 10 months in charge, Carrick replied: 'The game's certainly changed, no doubt. It always does. It had evolved to the stage probably after Sir Matt (Busby) in the years after that, to then Sir Alex.

'It evolves all the time. That's how it is. It changes. We've got to deal with what's in front of us. I get the comparisons. I understand it. There are always different things to compare, but it's we've always got to live in the present as well.

Michael Carrick accepts he won't be given time to emulate Sir Alex Ferguson at Man United

'Definitely use past experiences to help us, but we're all desperate to win football matches and win them as quickly as you possibly can.'

It was suggested that the 3-2 comeback win over Roma could be Carrick's 'Mark Robins moment' in reference to the striker's FA Cup winner against Nottingham Forest that was said to have saved Ferguson from the sack.

'More comparisons, yeah,' smiled Carrick. 'You always find something to compare, don't you? It's up to us to take it forward, you know. It was a fantastic night.

'It was nice for the supporters to end like that and to have that feeling, one hundred per cent for sure.

'But nothing's achieved yet, and it's one moment really in time that we need to use to move forward. But we need more of it, for sure.'

On Ferguson's 40th anniversary at United and his relationship with the Scot these days, Carrick said: 'What has not been said already? It's something definitely worth celebrating as a football club. That it's 40 years this week since he came here, and the impact on the football club, moving forward for all the time that he was here and that he's left for others to carry forward is incredible.

'I'm probably closer to him now and speak more to him now than I did when I was playing. I think the relationships change over time and that's only natural. Ever since I came here, the impact he had on me as an individual was immense.

'Not necessarily directly as in talking all the time and advice all the time. Personally, it was more the influence that he had and the habits that you try and pick up from him, as did everyone around the football club over that period of time. Then you move on and evolve a little bit into a friendship, which is really nice.'

Carrick confirmed that new £70million signing Carlos Baleba will be out for a 'number of weeks' after injuring his right hamstring against Roma, and United will wait to see if Patrick Dorgu can be involved against Villa after returning to training following a hamstring strain of his own.

Unai Emery has confirmed he intends to sign a new long-term deal with Aston Villa – ending Manchester United's hopes of hiring him if they sack Michael Carrick.

The Villa boss' current contract expires in 2029 and ahead of Sunday's meeting with United, Emery intends to extend the arrangement after positive talks with co-owner Nassef Sawiris.

'We were speaking about how we can set our project stronger with a [new] deal, yes,' he said. 'Nassef is very ambitious, a hard worker and his commitment to the club is huge. It's easy for me to have meetings with him as normally we are on the same page.

'It's about increasing our budget each year, and our revenue. Improving players, selling players, buying young talented players. This is the process and vision we should keep for the future.

'I had a very good, positive meeting with Nassef. Let's talk next week, trying to get everything that we have spoken about together.'

United's admiration for Emery has created a storm. Villa threatened to sue Sky Sports pundit Jamie Carragher for telling fans to 'shut the f*** up'. Supporters had objected to Carragher arguing that Emery should join United to further his career, and Carragher apologised for his crude language.

Villa's announcement on Wednesday that Sawiris and Emery had held talks also felt like a warning shot towards United.

Unai Emery has confirmed he intends to sign a new long-term deal with Aston Villa

Emery has never won at Old Trafford as Villa boss and despite United's poor form he remains wary.

He said: 'It's Manchester United. We haven't won one match there in the four years I've been here.

'It's very difficult for us. It's a fantastic club. They performed very well last year to get into the Champions League.

'This year in the league they've not been consistent at the start but they should recover. We played Barcelona in the Champions League on Tuesday and this game is of the same level.'

Enzo Maresca has not felt the need to dissect Gianluigi Donnarumma's troubling form with Manchester City's No 1 amid a number of high-profile errors.

Donnarumma was flat-footed in being beaten at his near post during Wednesday's Champions League win at RB Leipzig, following mistakes against Paris Saint-Germain and Aston Villa.

City's manager does not see the value in raking over the issues with his goalkeeper and believes the Italian's confidence is still high.

'He's good, overall he's good,' Maresca said of Donnarumma's mood. 'He's quite relaxed, in terms of recognising that he has done some mistakes but has made some good saves.

'There are players that when they make mistakes you need conversation with them, and there are players that when they make some mistake you just need eye contact to understand that he recognises.

'With Gigio it's quite easy because every time he has made some mistake or something that was not correct, he recognises immediately. Sometimes you don't need to be so deep with some players.'

Meanwhile, Maresca insisted that Elliot Anderson has another level to reach after an encouraging start to his City career.

Enzo Maresca has not felt the need to dissect Gianluigi Donnarumma's troubling form

The £116million summer signing goes back to Nottingham Forest on Saturday, with Maresca claiming that Oliver Glasner's side are in a false position.

'Elliot can do many [more] things, but he knows that,' Maresca added. 'We had a conversation before the last Champions League game, a nice conversation on understanding the game and slowly he is getting better and better.

'Elliot in this moment is playing more as the holding midfielder, but in future he can also play a little bit more up front.'

Fulham head coach Alvaro Arbeloa has backed Josh King to make the most of his 'special moment' after being called up to the England squad for the first time.

The 19-year-old has been selected by Thomas Tuchel for the upcoming Nations League games, having continued to impress this season after being part of England's pre-World Cup camp.

'It is a special moment for him and also for us as a club and our supporters. I think Josh is the pride of our club and supporters,' Arbeloa said at a press conference ahead of Saturday's Premier League game against Newcastle.

'He came to this club when he was eight and now you look at how he is playing and performing, so we are really proud of him. It is a big step for him and I am so happy for him because he is a very humble kid. You can see every day he wants to improve, to work and become a better player.'

King will again be central to Fulham's gameplan when Arbeloa's side take on Newcastle looking to build some momentum following last weekend's battling 1-1 draw at Aston Villa.

'For me, he is a very talented player. He can receive between the lines where there is no space,' Arbeloa said. 'He can drive, he can run, he can dribble with the ball, so he has everything, and we are working with him about his finishing.

'Obviously he needs to improve there and it is the most difficult thing in football, scoring goals, but his mentality, his character, he is a very mature player. You can't forget that he is just 19 years old, so it is unbelievable how he is performing. I think he has everything to become one of the best players in the world.'

After losing their last home game 1-0 to strugglers Crystal Palace, Arbeloa is expecting a response back at Craven Cottage. 'We know how difficult it will be to beat Newcastle tomorrow, they are a very good team, but we are playing well,' said the Fulham boss, who will be without defender Timothy Castagne because of a knee issue.

Thomas Tuchel tells Cole Palmer he 'misses too many opportunities' to play for England after leaving Chelsea star out of his latest squad

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By JAMES DUTTON, DEPUTY SPORTS NEWS EDITOR and KIERAN GILL, MAIL SPORT REPORTER

Published: 06:46 EDT, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 09:39 EDT, 6 November 2026

Thomas Tuchel has warned Cole Palmer he 'misses too many opportunities' to play for England, after leaving him out of his latest squad.

The Chelsea star is a high-profile omission ahead of upcoming Nations League games against Croatia and Spain, having withdrawn from duty during the last international break with a minor muscular injury.

England were assured Palmer is 100 per cent fit and ready when they contacted Chelsea this week – with his omission purely down to a selection decision from Tuchel and not to do with any fitness issues.

The German said on Friday: 'I guess everyone is desperate to play for England and Cole is no exception.

'We are speaking now about a player that is not in camp. It counts when you are in camp and I just said last time that he misses too many opportunities to impress.

'It was a long camp, it was two and a half weeks and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary.

Thomas Tuchel has warned Cole Palmer he misses too many opportunities to play for England

Palmer is a high-profile omission from Tuchel's squad for England's upcoming Nations League games

He withdrew from the last squad, leaving boss Thomas Tuchel questioning his commitment

'That's why the credit goes to them, the trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress.

'So it's not personal and of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country, but the competition is on and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.'

Last month Tuchel appeared to question Palmer's desire to represent his country, warning he 'misses way too many camps' as the 24-year-old skipped all four England fixtures in September and October.

Palmer had played a full 90 for Chelsea away at Bournemouth before that break, which he then used to fly abroad to the Caribbean with his girlfriend, Olivia Holder, after withdrawing from England duty.

He was accompanied by a personal rehab coach on that trip, and was provided with an injury-specific training and recovery plan by his club.

Palmer started in Chelsea's first fixture when the Premier League returned at home to Brentford with Blues boss Xabi Alonso insisting they were right to take care of their star player themselves.

It has been described as standard procedure for England to make medical checks on players with clubs. While it does not guarantee a call-up, it indicates how Tuchel has been weighing up offering Palmer another chance as he names his next squad on Friday morning.

Chelsea boss Xabi Alonso (right) said he had not spoken with Palmer about his England omission

Asked if he had spoken with Palmer about his England omission, Alonso said: 'We have not spoken about that. We have been talking about our game on Sunday. We are very happy with Cole.

'He's enjoying his game, he's connecting very well with the other attackers, he's helping the team. I'm very happy with his quality, his attitude, and his impact.

'We have not talked about that (England). We are focused on Chelsea things, how to do better, how to help him, how he can become more and more influential in the game.

'We speak about Chelsea. We speak about us. We have enough on our plate. My impact area is my control area, and that's what we talk about.

'He needs to focus on his thing. His thing is to play football, to enjoy his game, with us, with Chelsea, to connect with his team-mates.

'I would never tell (a player to withdraw). When a player is fully fit, he is selectable. When he is not fit, we try to give the national team the reasonable information to decide together, because sometimes it's not good for the player.

'For example, Anselmino, he has been called up for Argentina, they asked us, he has been training for the last 10 days better, and for him, it's very important to have his first call-up.'

'Surprising' Joe Taylor decision can hand Huddersfield Town major boost

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Welsh National Team squad for their two crucial Nations League Group Stage matchups against Norway and Portugal was revealed on Wednesday, and a key omission surprised League One fans.

Oxford United's Mark Harris has retained his place as one of the strikers in Craig Bellamy's squad.

The 27-year-old has been a consistent member of the Welsh squad under Bellamy, and with six goals in 13 league outings this season, he's been solid enough to warrant a place. However, when looking at Joe Taylor's output in the same division, it raises some questions.

The Huddersfield Town frontman has finally showcased his quality in front of goal, as he seemed to have done everywhere other than at the Accu Stadium this year, hitting double figures already after his strike on Tuesday night against Luton Town took him to 10.

Taylor now has 21 goals in just 34 league outings in 2026, both at Huddersfield and while on loan at Wigan Athletic in the second half of last season, and a lot of fans are questioning why he hasn't been called up.

It clearly isn't due to his status as a League One footballer, as someone in the same division with a lower goal output has got a place ahead of him. Harris is one of three third-tier players in the Welsh squad, alongside fellow Oxford man Kai Andrews, who is on loan from Coventry City, and Leicester City's Liam Cullen.

When asked about Taylor's omission, FLW's Huddersfield fan pundit, Graeme Rayner, appreciated that Harris has had a good season so far, but did find it slightly shocking that the Terriers' frontman hasn't at least joined him in the squad.

"It's surprising to me that he's been left out in one way, in the way that the guy who has been picked over him, while doing okay, is not doing as well as Joe Taylor," Graeme told Football League World.

"Mark Harris has six goals and one assist in League One, while Taylor has 10 goals and two assists this season, both having played 13 games and a similar amount of minutes, so Taylor is in better form, although both are doing well."

Rayner also brought up the fact that Taylor hasn't been given a chance to prove himself in the Welsh fold, having not seen the pitch after being a late call-up for their Nations League fixtures in the last break. Meanwhile, Harris has yet to score a goal for his country.

"Harris has 19 games for the Wales squad and hasn't scored, so I guess it's surprising in that aspect," Rayner continued. "But Taylor was called up for the last break and didn't get a sniff on the pitch, so maybe it's not that surprising.

"They are different players. Harris is a bigger unit. Taylor is short and powerful, but Harris is a six-foot striker, so I suppose there is a difference there."

Despite the quality throughout the squad of the current League One leaders, Huddersfield are one of the few sides not expected to have their game postponed over the break.

Third-tier sides have to have three players who fall under a playing quota to be called up to request postponement, and while Luton postponed their game over the recent break, their away matchup at Burton Albion went ahead without Taylor leading the line.

Martin Drury's side take on Wycombe Wanderers over this break, and will be doing so without star man Derensili Sanches Fernandes, who is universally agreed to be the best player in League One currently, as he was deservedly called up by Cape Verde for the first time.

Heading to Adams Park without both Sanches Fernandes and Taylor, who have 25 goal involvements between them, would be detrimental to their chances of winning, according to Rayner.

What do you think about Joe Taylor's omission from the latest Welsh squad? Let us know in the comments.

"I'd say it [not having Taylor on international duty] is a good thing for us, because he's in such good form," the fan pundit explained. "Derensili Sanches Fernandes has been called up, so we're going to be missing him, and Taylor is our other big, in-form player.

"So it's good that they're not both away, because we want to continue this momentum we have."

Huddersfield heading to Burton without Taylor was a blow during the last international break. What was even more frustrating was that Craig Bellamy's side would have had the same results without the 23-year-old in the squad, as he didn't play.

In the end, Martin Drury's side didn't miss their talisman too much (Burton did a good enough job to assist their goals anyway), but if they hadn't come away with that 3-1 win, there'd have been questions asked about whether things would have been different with Taylor.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

If the same happened again next week and the 23-year-old just rode the bench for Wales, there'd have been some real frustration among Huddersfield fans. Instead, he'll be available to try and extend their unbeaten league run to eight games.

Taylor's time for Wales will come. Those at the Accu Stadium will hope it's while they're a Championship club next year, so they don't have to worry about the 23-year-old missing any league games.

Sheffield Wednesday will hate fresh Wrexham update

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The decision on where Davis Keillor-Dunn will spend the rest of the season will be made in January, and it's between two options.

The 29-year-old attacker joined Sheffield Wednesday on loan in the dying hours of the summer transfer window from Wrexham, where even calling him a fringe player was a bit of a stretch.

He'd established himself as one of the best players in League One over the preceding 18 months before his January move to the Championship side, netting 31 times in 66 third-tier outings at Barnsley.

However, he played just 89 minutes in the second half of last season, with many neutrals and League One fans frustrated at the lack of involvement. He was destined for a return to the third tier this summer, but crucially, he played in the first two competitive games for Phil Parkinson's side this year, making Wednesday the second club he'd appeared for already this season.

Those two appearances in the EFL Cup and in the Championship opener against Cardiff City meant that, once Wrexham loaned Keillor-Dunn out to a different club, and he played there, he wouldn't be able to play for a third side this season.

Sheffield Wednesday secured his services but only on a loan deal until January. Usually, those moves can raise some alarm bells, as performing well at Hillsborough for five months could pique the interest of other Championship sides, leading the League One Owls to lose him midway through the season.

However, regardless of how well Keillor-Dunn performed in the first half of this season at Hillsborough, the only other EFL club he could play for this season was Wrexham, where he evidently wasn't favoured.

So, even though the initial spell ran out in the new year, there would have been some confidence that an agreement could be struck to keep him at Hillsborough post-January. That was, until a fresh injury concern at the Racecourse, which could see Keillor-Dunn kept on at the Championship club.

Keillor-Dunn was being kept out of the Wrexham side by the likes of Sam Smith, Callum O'Hare and Josh Windass, who all operate as the linking 10 to Kieffer Moore, who remains the focal point in Phil Parkinson's squad.

However, in a crushing blow, Smith is set to miss the next nine months after suffering a cruciate ligament injury during last Friday's loss against Sheffield United.

That, plus niggling injury issues which have kept last season's top scorer Windass unable to start more than a couple of games at a time, and the fact that O'Hare is currently absent, too, will make bringing in a new attacking midfielder a priority at the Racecourse in the January window.

Indeed, the Red Dragons played Lewis O'Brien behind Moore on Wednesday night against Middlesbrough, and while he chipped in with an assist, it's evident he won't be the long-term solution in that position.

With Keillor-Dunn naturally returning to Wrexham in January, perhaps they won't need to splash the cash significantly on bringing in someone to bolster that position — they could just utilise who they already have at the club.

Ultimately, that puts Sheffield Wednesday in a bit of a catch-22 situation. They'll want Keillor-Dunn to perform well for them to push them towards the top two around the turn of the new year, but if he does, Wrexham may feel more inclined to keep him instead of agreeing to extend the loan deal.

The 29-year-old netted his third league goal of the campaign against Blackpool, making it three in his last five, after he scored a brace against AFC Wimbledon.

If Keillor-Dunn keeps this scoring form up and pushes double digits by January, that'll be the second season running that he's showcased his quality in half a campaign with 10 or more goals before heading to Wrexham.
