If it wasn't clear already that the Wednesday loanee was good enough to play regular Championship minutes last January, it should be this coming winter window if he continues to perform.

It definitely wasn't a situation Sheffield Wednesday thought they'd be in, given how unfavoured Keillor-Dunn was at the Racecourse, but that injury to Smith has just opened the door slightly for the 29-year-old to be reintegrated back into Phil Parkinson's fold.

From a Wednesday perspective, they'll just be hoping that Wrexham decide that this slight injury crisis behind the striker will lead them to the transfer window, rather than to their loan star.\

UTSA 'manhandled' in road loss to FAU as American title hopes take a hit

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

BOCA RATON, Fla. - Leaving the field after a lopsided loss to Florida Atlantic, UTSA coach Jeff Traylor returned to a locker room that he described as disappointed, embarrassed and disgusted.

The Roadrunners entered the night on the fringe of the national top 25 with everything in front of them, remaining in strong position for an American Conference championship and a College Football Playoff berth for as long as they could keep their winning streak going.

Instead, UTSA was overpowered and outplayed on both sides of the ball, allowing the highest point total of Traylor's tenure in a 60-29 road rout Thursday.

"We didn't give our kids a great plan as a staff. They gave their guys a better plan than we did," Traylor said. "So, it starts with the staff, but up front, we got manhandled on both sides of the ball."

The loss drops the Roadrunners to 7-2 on the year with a 4-1 record in the American Conference, snapping a five-game winning streak. UTSA remains in contention for the league title, as North Texas is the lone unbeaten at 4-0, and Memphis is the only other one-loss team at 4-1.

But the path to a College Football Playoff spot as the highest-ranked team in the Group of Six became much more perilous. UTSA entered the night receiving votes in both the Associated Press and coaches polls, joining the conversation with Boise State and New Mexico as the best in the Group of Six. The Roadrunners will now need both of those teams and perhaps a few others to drop games down the stretch.

"It's a new challenge now," UTSA quarterback Owen McCown said. "We'll see how our team responds. I know they'll respond the right way."

UTSA's defense had been spotty in stretches all season, entering the night ranked just outside the top 100 nationally in total defense at 391.5 yards per game.

But the Roadrunners had usually managed to find success in one aspect or another, often taking away an opponent's biggest strength and forcing them to play out of rhythm. The Owls' up-tempo, air raid attack entered the night ranked in the top five in the country in passing and in the bottom 20 in rushing, but Traylor said he was "pretty shocked" to see how heavily the Owls committed to the run, as FAU exploded for 339 yards on the ground.

FAU averaged 6.9 yards per rush, led by running back Leonard Farrow with 12 carries for 126 yards and a score.

"We knew everything coming in," UTSA defensive lineman Jeremiah Bailey said. "They were more prepared, got the best of us tonight."

The Owls' passing attack was effective when needed, as well, racking up 224 yards as FAU became the first team to eclipse 200 yards both through the air and on the ground against UTSA this year.

BOCA RATON, FL - NOVEMBER 05: Florida Atlantic Owls tight end Antonio Johnson (1) rushes for a touchdown int he first half during the game between UTSA and FAU on Thursday, November 5, 2026 at Flagler Credit Union Stadium in Boca Raton, FL (Photo by Peter Joneleit/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images) (Icon Sportswire/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images)

Quarterback Caden Veltkamp hit 17 of 25 passes for 210 yards with four touchdowns, including three to star wideout Easton Messer.

"It always starts up front," Traylor said. "Their O-line manhandled us."

That physical disadvantage was evident on the other side, too, as Traylor said UTSA's offensive line "couldn't protect our quarterback."

McCown completed 20 of 35 passes for 265 yards with three touchdowns but was sacked six times as the Roadrunners faced consistent pressure.

"I have full trust in those guys up front on the O-line," McCown said. "I just don't think they played as good as they wanted to tonight, and that's all right. It's part of football. It's more important to see the response."

UTSA's rushing attack was also held to 136 yards at an average of 3.8 yards per carry, and FAU often pulled a safety up closer to the line of scrimmage to limit the Roadrunners' ground game.

That put more of a burden on UTSA's receivers to beat their defenders, and Traylor said the Roadrunners targets "weren't winning on press coverage," forcing McCown to hold onto the ball longer.

BOCA RATON, FL - NOVEMBER 05: *UTSA Roadrunners quarterback Owen McCown (2) makes a pass attempt in the first half during the game between UTSA and FAU on Thursday, November 5, 2026 at Flagler Credit Union Stadium in Boca Raton, FL (Photo by Peter Joneleit/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images) (Icon Sportswire/Icon Sportswire via Getty Images)

"We've got to get receivers open," Traylor said. "As a coaching staff, we've got to do a better job, and receivers have to get open and win some of those downfield throws. That's really it."

After having a few extra days to heal up through the open week, an array of Roadrunners went down to injuries on Thursday, and Traylor said UTSA "lost some great players tonight." The extent of most of the knocks remains unknown, Traylor said, but UTSA's ability to shuffle the depth chart where needed is "going to be the biggest deal" as the Roadrunners look to regroup.

North Texas will present a very similar challenge, entering the week ranked atop the FBS in total offense at 539.6 yards per game. A win vaults UTSA back into a first-place tie atop the American, and McCown expects the group to answer the challenge.

"I hope as a team this puts a little chip on our shoulder going into a really big game next week," McCown said. "We're still in this hunt. It's the top of the conference, but we're having to play a really good team next week in the Dome in North Texas. So, we have to respond the right way. I know we will."

This article originally published at UTSA 'manhandled' in road loss to FAU as American title hopes take a hit.

Two sides eager to end this mini block of fixtures with a positive ahead of the international break face off at the Hawthorns on Saturday, as West Brom welcome Millwall.

The Lions have just one win in their last five, and found themselves 3-0 down inside half an hour at Birmingham City in midweek. Alex Neil's side will need to be switched on immediately to avoid consecutive defeats before the international break.

Jesus Owono Update Emerges Days After Jesus de Miguel Death

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Rob is senior football editor at GiveMeSport, with over 15 years experience covering sport in various capacities since graduating from the University of Gloucestershire in 2008 with a degree in journalism. A childhood obsession for all things football developed into a career writing about the beautiful game, initially producing content for clients including Luton Town Football Club and the PGMOL. His first big interviews were with Premier League referees Howard Webb and Michael Oliver in 2009. He then spent four years producing written content for a betting website, mainly focusing on football, and was there to cover various games and tournaments including the 2010 World Cup and Euro 2012. Rob joined GiveMeSport in 2013 (initially as a sub-editor before becoming a senior writer and then senior editor in 2020) and has covered countless football matches and other sporting events, interviewed various high-profile figures including Rio Ferdinand, Peter Crouch and Theo Walcott, and written well over 10,000 articles during that time. Rob has played a key role in GiveMeSport's coverage of the World Cups in 2014, 2018 and 2022, plus Euro 2016, Euro 2020 and Euro 2024, along with many Champions League finals. While football is Rob's area of expertise, he has also covered a wide range of other sports during his time at GiveMeSport including boxing, tennis and darts, as well as the 2016, 2020 and 2024 Olympic Games.

A fresh update has emerged over Jesus Owono, the goalkeeper involved in the on-field collision that tragically led to CD Tenerife forward Jesus de Miguel’s death earlier this week.

A pathology report has revealed that De Miguel, who was only 30 years old, died from “accidental chest trauma” following the collision which occurred towards the end of Monday’s LaLiga 2 clash between Tenerife and Andorra.

Despite the best efforts of medical staff, the Spaniard’s death was announced on Tuesday morning.

Tenerife’s players and club staff, along with majority shareholder Rayco Garcia, attended De Miguel’s funeral at the San Miguel Arcangel church on Thursday.

Spain head coach Luis de la Fuente, Real Madrid president Florentino Perez, Atletico Madrid chief executive Miguel Angel Gil Marin and Spanish FA president Rafael Louzan were also among those in attendance.

"Feeling so much affection for Jesus ​and seeing the impact he had on so many people is something ⁠we will always carry with us," De Miguel’s family ⁠said in a statement.

"Jesus leaves a void that is impossible to fill, but also cherished memories for all of us ‌who were fortunate enough to share our lives with him. Thank you for loving him and for standing by us."

An investigation has been opened into De Miguel’s death, while Andorra’s goalkeeper Owono said in a statement: “These are the most difficult days of my life.”

"I struggle to find the words, and I find it very hard to ​process what has happened,” the 25-year-old, who has closed his Instagram account after receiving abusive message, said. “I know there will be many questions to address, and I will face whatever comes with the utmost respect.

"But right now, honestly, ​I cannot think about anything other than Jesus and the ⁠pain we are experiencing. Rest in ⁠peace, Jesus."

Owono will miss Equatorial Guinea’s upcoming AFCON 2027 qualifiers after asking to be left out of the squad while he comes to terms with the situation.

A new report from Spanish outlet Marca reveals Owono has been “destroyed” by De Miguel’s death and is currently surrounded by his mother, family and former teammates who have driven to his house to be by his side.

Owono has “not set foot in the street” after De Miguel’s death was announced and “has cried and continues to cry every moment”.

Among those reportedly helping Owono through this immensely difficult time is Barcelona and Spain legend Gerard Pique, co-owner of FC Andorra, who is personally involved.

Meanwhile, Andorra’s fans have also offered their support to their goalkeeper. An open letter published by Marca, and translated into English, reads:

“From the FC Andorra animation stand we want to send you all our support and affection in these hard and difficult times. We are aware of the suffering you are going through after what happened. We know that these are not easy days, but we want you to keep one thing in mind: you are not alone. By your side you have your family, your friends, your colleagues, the staff and the whole club. But you also have us, a hobby that appreciates you, respects you and that today, more than ever, wants to give you all their love and support.

“We also want you to know that we flatly reject all the insults, threats and judgments you are receiving these days. An accident does not define a person, much less his way of being. We don't want pain, anger or helplessness to turn into hatred towards you. Behind the soccer player there is a person, with feelings and with a family that is also suffering.

“We want you to know that here is your house. That when you go out on the field again, to defend our goal and wear this shirt, you will have a stand that will continue to be by your side, encouraging you as always. Now you don't have to prove anything to anyone. It won't be easy and it will take time, but even if it costs and hurts you have to get ahead, you have to work to be able to recover that claw and smile that characterises you so much. The most important thing now is your recovery, but along the way you should know that you can count on us, that you are not alone.”

He's Keen: Leeds Preparing to Reignite Interest in Harvey Elliott in January

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Leeds United remain interested in signing Liverpool midfielder Harvey Elliott after missing out on him over the summer, and the Yorkshire club are confident that a deal could be struck in January, according to Football Insider.

Elliott is currently on loan at Valencia, joining the Spanish club on transfer deadline day at the start of September after it was made clear to him that he isn't part of Andoni Iraola's plans at Anfield. The 23-year-old has made six appearances for Los Che so far, failing to contribute to a goal whilst being sent off in his last outing against Villarreal, which led to him receiving a 0/10 rating from one publication.

Valencia aren't said to be impressed with their loanee, and are reportedly planning to terminate his temporary spell in Eastern Spain once the winter window opens.

Leeds are preparing to swoop for Elliott, who is understood to be keen on a permanent move to another Premier League club.

Elliott has endured a turbulent few years since Jurgen Klopp left Liverpool. The creative midfielder was a regular under the German, making 53 appearances in the celebrated manager's final season on Merseyside.

However, Arne Slot was far less fond of the Fulham academy graduate, using him sparingly in his first season as Reds boss. The Dutchman granted Elliott just 18 Premier League appearances in the 2024/25 season, before sending him out on loan to Aston Villa for the duration of the following campaign.

Elliott had a miserable time in the West Midlands, appearing just nine times under Unai Emery, who effectively froze him out for much of the season.

He returned to Liverpool in the summer acutely aware that he wasn't wanted by new manager Iraola, but he only found an escape route from Anfield at the eleventh hour of the window, joining Valencia on loan.

Leeds were heavily linked with Elliott, but the Whites weren't able to agree a deal with the Premier League giants. Daniel Farke and the recruitment team at Elland Road haven't given up hope of landing the playmaker, however.

Football Insider report that Leeds are optimistic about their prospects of signing Elliott in January. They continue to monitor his situation at Valencia, which isn't said to be harmonious.

Elliott's Premier League Statistics 2023/24 (Last Season Under Klopp)

The Englishman is out of contract at Liverpool in the summer of 2028, meaning Leeds should have leverage in negotiations this winter, particularly if Valencia actively choose to offload him prematurely.

Farke's side have enjoyed an encouraging start to the new season, although they certainly lack firepower in forward areas, scoring just nine goals in nine matches so far.

Elliott would help alleviate this issue, with his ability to create chances in the final third using his inventive passing an attribute that is sorely lacking in Leeds' current squad.

Arsenal are among the favourites as they look to finally win a major European honour.

Derby County could turn to ex-Premier League boss if they sack John Eustace, report claims

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Derby County have won one of their first 14 Championship fixtures this season and their head coach Eustace is under serious pressure. DCFC are in the bottom three along with Preston North End and Burnley and they are seven points from safety behind Watford and Charlton Athletic.

Eustace, 47, has been in charge of the Rams since February 2025 and prior to this campaign, the ex-Birmingham City and Blackburn Rovers did an eye-catching job at Pride Park. However, things haven’t gone to plan over recent times and he may be sacked if things don’t improve.

In this latest update regarding the managerial situation, Football Insider claim Cooper has been sounded out already and ‘preliminary’ talks have been held with him regarding a possible move. The 46-year-old has been available since his exit from Brondby in late May and he has been weighing up his options over the past five months.

Derby County lost 4-0 at home against Norwich City last time out. They are back in action on Friday night with another match on their own patch against Bolton Wanderers.

Cooper is an experienced manager and knows the EFL well. He has overseen just under 350 games from the dugout so far in his career.

The Welshman could help steady the ship at Derby County if he joined them. DCFC need someone to tighten up their defensive department.

Cooper landed the Swansea City job in 2019 after working in the England national team youth set-up and he got the Swans to the play-off final before later ending up at Nottingham Forest. He then managed to get the Reds promoted to the Premier League in 2022.

His more recent spells at Leicester City and Brondby haven’t quite gone to plan. Therefore, he may feel he has a point to prove now.

Derby County have to beat Bolton Wanderers as they stare down the barrel of relegation down to League One. If they fail to do so, Eustace is likely to be given the chop by DCFC.

Cooper would be a good alternative and a decent coup by the Rams if they went for him, regardless of his past Nottingham Forest connections. He has proven himself in the second tier before and has also worked in the top flight so must have something about him.

“We Need to Earn it” Scott Parker’s Simple Message To West Ham Supporters

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham manager Scott Parker has wasted little time attempting to reconnect the football club with its supporters, and I have to say, I like what I’m hearing. Speaking ahead of Saturday’s Championship fixture against Blackburn Rovers, the new Hammers boss made it clear that the players and coaching staff have a responsibility to earn the backing of the fans.

Much has been made of Parker’s previous association with West Ham, which is understandable given his four excellent years at the club. However, I don’t necessarily believe a manager needs to have played for the Hammers to understand what makes the place special.

I’ve spoken quite a lot recently about small wins and easy wins, and this is another perfect example. Sometimes it doesn’t require an elaborate public relations exercise to make supporters feel appreciated; it simply requires somebody to demonstrate that they understand the football club.

Speaking during his first pre-match press conference, Parker acknowledged that supporters cannot simply be expected to turn up and applaud regardless of what happens on the pitch.

“You need to earn that, and we need to earn that. We can’t expect our fans just to come along and do that.”

He also promised to do everything possible to bring the club together, recognising the importance of the connection between the players and supporters. It’s an encouraging message, particularly after a difficult period in which the relationship between the team and the fanbase has suffered.

I remember when Alan Pardew arrived at West Ham without any previous association with the club. He promised to delve into the Hammers’ history books and learn what West Ham was all about, which struck me as exactly the right approach.

One gesture that has always stuck in my mind was Pardew sending his players out to warm up wearing T-shirts bearing the words “Moore than just a football club”. It was a classy nod towards Bobby Moore and demonstrated that you don’t necessarily need a West Ham playing career to understand the importance of the club’s history.

Noble joined Parker in the West Ham dugout

There was another of those small wins following West Ham’s extraordinary comeback against Cardiff City on Wednesday night. Parker made a point of going over to the travelling supporters and applauding them at the final whistle.

It might seem a relatively insignificant gesture, but I don’t think it is. Parker understands what West Ham means to its supporters, and rather than simply talking about that connection, he’s demonstrating it through his actions.

Scotty P has already spoken enthusiastically about working alongside his former teammate Nobes, highlighting their shared values and the enormous respect Mark Noble commands at the club.

Of course, none of this guarantees success, and ultimately Parker will be judged on whether he can get West Ham promoted. But when approximately 7,000 supporters are preparing to travel to Blackburn, acknowledging their commitment is a pretty good place to start.

I’ve always believed there are certain things a manager can do immediately to improve the atmosphere around a football club. Parker and Noble appear to understand that, and if they can combine those small gestures with the sort of second-half performance we witnessed at Cardiff, they’ll go a long way towards winning supporters over.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

Scott Parker connection will have £20m West Ham player fearing for his future

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham are embarking on a new era under Scott Parker after the former player was named as Nuno Espírito Santo’s successor.

The aim for the 46-year-old is clear - he has to lead the Hammers back to the Premier League this season.

And, despite inheriting a team that isn’t in the top two, Parker will certainly believe automatic promotion is possible, and his pedigree suggests he is the man to do the job, as he has taken Fulham, Bournemouth and Burnley up, achieving 100 points with the latter.

So, despite some concerns over the style of play, Parker looks like a good fit, and he is working with a group of players that many consider to be the best in the league.

It’s hard to argue with that when you see the likes of Konstantinos Mavropanos, Arne Engels, Manor Solomon, and, of course, Jarrod Bowen in the group, and Parker will be tasked with getting those players, and others, performing to their best level.

Another area of the pitch that West Ham do have quality is in goal, although it’s fair to say that Mads Hermansen and Alphonse Areola haven’t shown that consistently in the past 18 months.

On paper, both should be excellent options at this level, as Danish international Hermansen has won the Championship with Leicester, and he boasts ability on the ball as well as good reflexes.

Meanwhile, Areola has had a career that has seen him turn out for PSG, Real Madrid and France, so, at 33, he should be more than good enough for the Championship.

Yet, as outlined above, for whatever reason, since Hermansen arrived, the duo have not been anywhere near their best.

We all know the importance of having a good number one, and it’s one of the biggest decisions facing Parker.

So, both will be eager to impress, but one factor that may give Areola the edge is that he has played for Parker before during his time with Fulham.

Whilst it was an ultimately disappointing year for the stopper, as the Cottagers were relegated, Areola enhanced his reputation with his performances in the capital.

The keeper, who was signed by Parker, kept nine clean sheets in 36 top flight games, which is a very respectable return, and he was named as Fulham’s Player of the Year.

Whoever Parker does pick as his number one, it would make sense to give them a run in the team.

Of course, performances over a lengthy period will dictate who deserves the shirt, but constantly changing keepers doesn’t do anybody any favours.

There can be a temptation to make a switch when two players are of a similar level, and it can be argued it drives standards.

Yet, Areola and Hermansen have different attributes, and continuity can be important for a defence, who will want a reliable stopper behind them.

So, Parker must be prepared to give his first-choice a run in the team, and he will hope that whoever it is repays him with some big performances.

Following on from that, West Ham are unlikely to keep the duo happy, as they will both understandably feel they should be playing week in, week out at this level.

From the perspective of the club, it could be argued that Hermansen should play, as he was a big investment, he is younger, and he is under contract for four more years.

Who should Scott Parker pick as his West Ham number one? Let us know in the comments below.

As for Areola, his deal is up at the end of the season, and considering he will be 34 by the time that expires, he may not get a new contract on the same wages.

Hermansen will be hoping the long-term view means he retains his place in the XI, but Parker’s connection with Areola will be a concern.

Ultimately, it’s a huge decision that Parker has to make straight away, and it’s one that he must get right if the Hammers are to win promotion this season.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Opinion: He's the one, South American superstar just showed he's ready to join Liverpool

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool's next signing should now be obvious.

Andoni Iraola has just entered stormy days at Anfied. But he will walk through it. Given the early signs he has shown in his Liverpool career, there is optimism that a golden light awaits at the end.

Liverpool have always practised patience with their managers. Jurgen Klopp was given plenty of time to rebuild his squad. Even Arne Slot, who seemed to lose all grip and control was given an entire season to rectify his errors.

Iraola will have the courtesy to do the same. Things didn't get off to the perfect start for him at Bournemouth either.

But then in his second season, he delivered the club's best ever finish in the Premier League and subsequently led them to European football for the first time.

Iraola's mission to rebuild Liverpool counts on one manAt Anfield, Iraola has encountered a new challenge. Previously, he has been in charge of underdogs and he has been tasked with winning against the odds. Conversely, in his new role the expectation is already there for Liverpool to be winning matches and major trophies, too. So while not winning his first nine games at Bournemouth counted as a crisis there, losing two games in a row counts as a crisis for Liverpool. Undoubtedly, Iraola's mission to rebuild Liverpool will heavily rely on the club's big money acquisitions. None more so than Florian Wirtz. The Reds have tried to build a team around the German. They have given him runners to lay off passes to like Bradley Barcola and a midfielder who can get the ball to his feet in Trey Nyoni.

Bolton Wanderers midfielder’s rise from League Two to Championship regular deserves more recognition: Opinion

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bolton Wanderers have made a decent start to life back in the Championship this season, sitting 17th after 14 games. During this start, a real standout has been experienced midfielder Josh Sheehan.

Sheehan has quietly built an impressive career through consistency, technical quality and reliability. At 31, the Welshman is now an established Championship midfielder at Bolton Wanderers, having worked his way up from the demands of League Two.

His journey has not followed the usual route of a highly rated academy graduate becoming a first-team regular. After coming through Swansea City’s academy, which he joined at the age of 10, Sheehan struggled to establish himself in senior football and spent time on loan at Yeovil Town before finding his feet at Newport County.

Newport proved an important stepping stone. He became a dependable figure in their midfield, winning the club’s Player of the Year award in 2019/20 before earning a place in the PFA League Two Team of the Year the following campaign.

Bolton offered him a fresh opportunity in the summer of 2021, signing him on a free transfer. Sheehan quickly became an important player, but his 2023/24 campaign was arguably his finest. He registered 12 league assists, collected both the club’s Player of the Season and Players’ Player of the Season awards, and was named in the PFA League One Team of the Year.

The step up to the Championship has done little to diminish his importance. Sheehan has continued to provide Steven Schumacher with a reliable midfield presence and has started virtually every league fixture in 2026/27.

According to FotMob, his underlying numbers demonstrate that he offers considerably more than simple dependability. With approximately 85 per cent passing accuracy, around 17 chances created and roughly 2.5 expected assists, he remains an effective distributor.

He has also contributed two goals, demonstrating his willingness to shoot from distance and arrive in dangerous positions.

Sheehan is not necessarily the modern, physically dominant midfielder or an explosive box-to-box presence. Due to his smaller stature, the Welsh international has had to learn how best to use his abilities and game by game you can see that as he appears to stroll through games.

His strengths lie in controlling possession, delivering accurate long passes, taking set-pieces and keeping Bolton’s midfield functioning. His availability is another major asset, particularly in a demanding Championship campaign.

His repeated contract extensions, with his current agreement running until 2028, underline Bolton’s appreciation of those qualities. His growing involvement with Wales under Craig Bellamy is further recognition of his development.

Sheehan might never attract the attention reserved for more glamorous midfielders, but his career demonstrates that reliability, technical ability and sustained improvement still count for plenty. Bolton have a midfielder who has earned his place at every level, and that deserves considerably more recognition.

Arsenal transfer news: Gunners join Barcelona in chase for Serie A sensation

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Axel Clody | 06 Nov 2026 06:45

In the last summer transfer window, Benfica decided to part with Gustavo Varela, selling him to Monza for £1.7m (€2m) plus bonuses linked to his performances. The Portuguese giants did not include a buy-back clause in the deal, but secured 20% of any future capital gain on his sale.

It is a decision the Lisbon club may regret in the future, but it could also bring them a handsome cheque.

According to A Bola, the 21-year-old striker is beginning to attract interest from top European clubs. Several Premier League clubs, including Arsenal, have already shown an interest in Gustavo Varela.

Napoli are also following the Portuguese striker’s situation closely, while Barcelona have also been added to the list of interested clubs.

It is an impressive list of suitors for a player who came through the Sporting academy. That is hardly surprising, as he has been impressive at the start of this season.

Gustavo Varela has scored nine goals and provided one assist in nine Serie A matches. He is currently the league’s top scorer, ahead of Donyell Malen and his eight goals. The Portugal Under-21 international also scored twice in his only Coppa Italia match.

Monza are certainly aware they have hit the jackpot by signing Gustavo Varela for just £1.7m (€2m) this summer.

The Italian club are in a position of strength, with their striker’s contract running until June 2030. According to A Bola, the Serie A side are looking for a transfer fee in the region of £34m-£42.5m (€40m-€50m).

The player himself could also see his value rise if he is called up to the Portugal national team.

Jorge Jesus will announce his squad for the November international break on Friday, with a place in attack potentially available because of the situation surrounding Cristiano Ronaldo.

Messi deserves MLS MVP award, says Miami boss Gonzalez

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Inter Miami head coach Cristian 'Kily' Gonzalez believes Lionel Messi "deserves the MVP and also the Ballon d'Or" for his performances at 39 years of age.

Messi was on the scoresheet again last weekend as Miami defeated Eastern Conference rivals New England Revolution 2-1 on the road.

The win saw the Herons move back up to second in the East, one point ahead of the Revs, as they now prepare to host Charlotte FC on Decision Day.

Messi has scored more goals than any other player in MLS this season, with 26, while only Evander (17) and Miami team-mate Rodrigo De Paul (16) have provided more assists (15).

"I have no doubt that he's the best of all," Gonzalez said of Messi.

"None of them can compare to him when it comes to respect for his colleagues.

"Leo at his age and what he does, from my point of view, deserves the MVP and also the Ballon d'Or. I hope he can achieve it."

Messi's goal against New England also clinched Miami's spot in the 2027 Champions Cup.

"It makes me very happy for the players and for the club, we are on the side of contributing things," added Gonzalez.

"We are at a club that is in continuous growth, I have players committed to us. We needed to win and we challenged ourselves on what kind of team we wanted to be."

Revisiting this masterpiece ahead of Saturday…  #MIAvCLT https://t.co/NwenNe59wG

— Inter Miami CF (@InterMiamiCF) November 5, 2026

Charlotte are fifth in the East and already know they will face the Chicago Fire in the first round of the play-offs.

They thrashed Orlando City SC 4-0 last time out, but Henry Klesser, who scored in that win, is wary of Miami's strengths.

"Obviously a very difficult matchup," Kessler said. "They've got a lot of talented guys, so we'll have to be prepared, but I think it's going to take a team effort."

Messi failed to contribute to a goal in Miami's last home match, ending a run of 12 straight home starts with a goal contribution dating back to April.

Messi was directly involved in 23 goals (14 goals, nine assists) over that run.

Rodolfo Aloko and Abdoulaye Doucoure both came off the bench to score for Charlotte in their win over Orlando.

They are the third pair of team-mates to score as substitutes in a single match for Charlotte, and the first since Kerwin Vargas and Patrick Agyemang netted against New England in September 2024.

Miami are unbeaten in eight straight home matches in all competitions (W4 D4) after a three-match losing run in August.

The Herons have scored in 36 straight home matches in all competitions dating back to the Leagues Cup in 2025 at Chase Stadium, last failing to score at home against FC Cincinnati in July 2025.

Charlotte have posted 4-0 wins in each of their last two matches, over CF Montreal and Orlando, both at home. This is the 17th time in MLS history a team have won consecutive games by four or more goals, though no team have done so in three straight.

The Crown kept clean sheets in each of their last two meetings with Miami, including a scoreless draw in the last meeting in March. The only team to keep clean sheets in three straight regular-season meetings with Miami was Nashville SC in 2020-21.

Oregon football's season hasn't worked out the way it expected. But the Ducks (5-3, 3-2 Big Ten) were ranked in the first College Football Playoff rankings.

Rennes: Estéban Lepaul close to a return

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Good news for Stade Rennais, which could soon find Estéban Lepaul. Away from the field since October 18 due to an internal sprain of the left ankle contracted against Paris FC, the French striker is getting closer to a return. According to West Francethe Rennes striker should return to training this Thursday 5 or Friday 6 November, after more than two weeks of absence. The player notably missed the matches against Juventus (2-2) and Olympiakos (0-0) in the Europa League, as well as the matches against Strasbourg (3-0) and Nice (2-5) in Ligue 1. A significant absence for the Breton club, since Lepaul had scored seven goals in the league before his injury. Despite their desire to return quickly, the Rennes staff preferred to take precautions to avoid any relapse.

If his return to training is becoming clearer, his participation in the Lille reception on Sunday (3 p.m.) remains very uncertain. The time frame seems too short to consider tenure, or even a presence in the group. The most likely scenario would therefore be to take advantage of the international break to gradually regain rhythm and prepare for his return to competition. This injury could also compromise his chances of returning to the French team. While Zinédine Zidane must unveil his second list at the head of the Blues this Friday, November 6, Lepaul seems unlikely to be called up after several weeks without playing. A possible disappointment for the Rennes striker, who will probably have to wait before hoping for a new opportunity in the selection.

Lakers hire Will Iger, son of new co-owner Bob Iger, as next G League announcer

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Los Angeles Lakers have a new announcer for their G League affiliate. Fans might recognize his last name.

Will Iger, the son of incoming Lakers co-owner Bob Iger, will take over play-by-play duties for the Coachella Valley Lakers, the team announced Thursday. He will work alongside color commentator A.C. Green, who won three NBA titles with the Lakers during his playing career.

The elder Iger purchased a stake in the elder Lakers alongside venture capitalist Joshua Kushner at a valuation of $12.5 billion less than three months ago.

You can see a sample of the younger Iger's work below from his Instagram account.

Per the Lakers, Will Iger has worked at a number of stops in recent years, including for the G League Austin Spurs, San Antonio FC of the second-tier USL Championship soccer league, and both Columbia and Hofstra. He graduated from the University of Texas-Austin in 2025.

The sale of the Lakers to Kushner and Iger was reported in August, with the pair acquiring a majority stake in the team from owner Mark Walter amid a series of financial and legal difficulties for the billionaire. The pair could end up owning the entire team, as they have made an offer for the Buss family's remaining Lakers stake that every sibling wants to accept, save for team governor Jeanie Buss. How that works out is now a matter for the courts.

The sale of the Lakers is still pending, but it was reported in late October that Walter and Kushner, who is covering the vast majority of the purchase price, had reached an agreement on all outstanding terms of sale. The next step will be a review by the NBA, followed by a vote by the league's board of governors.

LEGO’s 2026 Holiday Ad Stars Daft Punk, E.T. & ‘KPop Demon Hunters’: Shop the Sets Featured in the Video

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

LEGO's latest holiday ad encourages families to spend more quality time together through hands-on play.

All products and services featured are independently chosen by editors. However, Billboard may receive a commission on orders placed through its retail links, and the retailer may receive certain auditable data for accounting purposes.

LEGO brings some familiar faces — and helmets — together for its 2026 holiday commercial.

Directed by The Greatest Showman filmmaker Michael Gracey, the ad brings together Daft Punk, E.T. the Extra-Terrestrial, Derpy Tiger from KPop Demon Hunters, Thor, Doctor Doom, the Ninjago Twin Titans and more in an action-packed video set to Daft Punk’s “One More Time.”

In the ad, one humble brick becomes the lead character in a fast-moving journey through a workshop full of familiar faces from film, music, sports and the wider LEGO universe, with a message about the simple pleasure of play. Related The Best Phone Plans for Frequent Concertgoers That Offer VIP Access, Ticket Discounts & Festival Lounges Netflix Rolls Out More Than An Hour of 'KPop Demon Hunters' Bonus Material, Including Playlists, Short-Form Stories and Games Carrie Coon Dubs Taylor Swift 'So Bertha,' While Praising Singer's Spot-On Impression of her 'Gilded Age' Character Explore Daft Punk See latest videos, charts and news

At the heart of this year’s campaign is interactive play with family and friends, and builds on last year’s holiday ad, which featured a child distracted by doomscrolling before LEGO characters inspired him to put down his phone and join his sister in building. This year, the message is all about starting with a single brick and the endless possibilities that come from hands-on creativity, working together and quality time.

According to research shared in LEGO’s press release, 89% of people wish they spent more time playing together as a family, while 24% of parents admit they’ve purchased a LEGO set as a gift knowing they would enjoy playing with it themselves. The findings give the campaign a timely angle, particularly for families looking for ways to spend more meaningful time together during the holidays.

LEGO is extending the campaign beyond the screen, too. Brick-and-mortar stores worldwide have been transformed into holiday workshops, offering festive experiences through Pick a Brick, Build A Mini, Minifigure Factory and the LEGO #BuildToGive initiative, which donates sets to children every year during the holidays.

For shoppers looking to recreate moments from the commercial, several featured sets are available to add to a holiday wish list, such as the Ferrari F2004 & Michael Schumacher build, which includes 735 pieces and costs $89.99. E.T. the Extra-Terrestrial, who made a memorable cameo in the ad, is made up of 1,226 pieces and is selling for $139.99, while the Avengers: Doomsday Quinjet is a 433-piece set priced at $59.99.

KPop Demon Hunters‘ Derpy Tiger and Sussie Bird made a brief appearance and together make up an 825-piece build for $69.99. Among the stars was also Lionel Messi, whose LEGO set comprises 958 pieces for $79.99. Together, these builds bring the commercial’s mix of entertainment, sports and nostalgia into the home.

LEGO’s holiday message is ultimately about more than what’s underneath the Christmas tree. It’s about making time to build, play and connect. As the ad’s outro reads: “Give the joy of bricks.”

Cavs reportedly sign Norchad Omier to two-way deal; waive Riley Minix

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Cleveland Cavaliers have signed G League standout Norchad Omier to a two-way deal. The organization has released 26-year-old wing Riley Minix in order to do so.

Omier previously had agreed to join the Minnesota Timberwolves but has reportedly changed his mind and plans on signing with the Cavaliers instead.

Cleveland's developmental league has done a fine job in recent years. Sam Merrill, Craig Porter Jr. and Nae'Qwan Tomlin are recent examples of players who have climbed the ranks to sign standard contracts and play rotational minutes in the NBA.

Omier will look to follow the same path. He joins Cleveland's other two-way prospects, Ernest Udeh Jr. and Tristan Enaruna.

Minix, who signed a two-way deal with the Cavs in February of this year. The lanky forward never found his outside shot in limited opportunities with Cleveland.

The Cavs have a need for someone like Omier. Now more than ever. Bulky forwards don't grow on trees, apparently, and the Cavs would be thrilled to have another player rapidly graduate from the G League to the NBA this season.

All of this is easier said than done. Omier is not a polished player and isn't a threat to shoot from the perimeter. The 6'7" forward shot 20% from deep in 11 games with the Charge last season. But he has the size and athleticism to fill a momentary need for the Cavaliers — who are dealing with various injuries to a roster that already lacked wing depth.

Omier will have an opportunity now to continue proving himself in the G League, with a chance of earning minutes at the NBA level. This is what every prospect in his position is striving for. It's clear that he likes his chances in Cleveland, considering he backed out of a commitment with Minnesota to be here. The same can be said for the Cavs, who swiftly opened a two-way spot to get him.

The Cavs play two games at home this weekend. First, against the Philadelphia 76ers on Friday. Then, the New York Knicks on Saturday. We'll see if any of the two-way guys get a chance to play.

Premier League: 10 things to look out for this weekend

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Lampard appreciation at Everton, Glasner sparking Forest fire, Zubimendi and Kroupi in spotlight and Carrick v Emery

Nothing better encapsulates the dysfunction that was Everton under Farhad Moshiri than the idea that an unknown vandal with a can of spray paint could influence the club’s managerial direction. “Pereira out, Lampard in” they daubed on a wall at Goodison Park when it appeared Vítor Pereira was in line to replace Rafael Benítez in 2022. Alarmed by fan protests, the Everton board opted instead for Frank Lampard and, while his tenure ended 12 months later with the club immersed in another relegation battle, there will be appreciation for the Coventry manager when he makes a first visit to Hill Dickinson Stadium on Friday. The connection between Lampard and Evertonians was genuine and a factor in keeping the club up in 2022. Both parties will be glad to see the other emerging stronger from those troubled times. Jack Grealish, meanwhile, will again be absent for Everton after his recent disciplinary breach, although the manager, David Moyes, said: “We will be working again with Jack soon.” Andy Hunter

Everton v Coventry City, Friday 8pm (all times GMT)

Back in September, when Leeds thrashed Newcastle 4-1 at Elland Road, talk of European qualification filled the West Yorkshire airwaves. Since then Daniel Farke’s side have drawn three and lost two of their ensuing Premier League games, scoring just two goals along the way. Given that their final pass has persistently let them down, optimism is turning to pessimism. Farke would call it realism. The German – who could probably do without facing Roberto De Zerbi’s improving Tottenham on Saturday – is confident his side are improving but knows they cannot expect to run before properly mastering the art of walking. “We don’t have the individual quality [of the Premier League’s leading teams],” conceded Farke on Thursday. “We have to compensate by being tactically spot on and good at winning second balls. It’s not a criticism of my players, it’s reality. The dream of Europe is allowed but we know our main aim is to reach 40 points as quickly as possible.” Louise Taylor

Martín Zubimendi has yet to start a Premier League game this season after falling down the pecking order at Arsenal and the Spain midfielder is likely to be the subject of speculation that he could seek to depart in January. The emergence of Myles Lewis-Skelly as an alternative in the No 6 position at the end of last season has had an impact on Zubimendi after he had been a regular starter for most of his debut campaign in the Premier League. The 27-year-old signed from Real Sociedad for around £55m 15 months ago and Mikel Arteta has insisted he remains an integral part of his plans. But with the Arsenal manager trying to find the right balance in his midfield after the arrival of Bruno Guimarães, Zubimendi will be hoping he gets another chance to stake his claim against Hull on Saturday after starting in the narrow win over Slavia Prague in midweek. Ed Aarons

Álvaro Arbeloa is proof that a struggling young manager working in England for the first time can bounce back after a difficult start. Arbeloa was sliding towards the sack a month ago but he has responded at Fulham, who have taken seven points from their last three games, and will hope to extend the unbeaten run when Matthias Jaissle’s inconsistent Newcastle visit Craven Cottage. It is an inviting fixture for Fulham and a big one for Newcastle, who were poor when they lost to Everton on Monday. Jaissle, hired as Eddie Howe’s replacement in the summer, is already under pressure. But the 38-year-old could take inspiration from how Arbeloa has given himself breathing space at Fulham. Jacob Steinberg

Oliver Glasner has previous. He led Wolfsburg from mid-table to the Champions League but fell out with the club hierarchy and left in 2021. Glasner next guided Eintracht Frankfurt to Europa League glory but was openly critical of the squad’s imbalance and quality in four separate outbursts and departed in 2023. His time at Crystal Palace was a great success but after accusing the Eagles’ board in January of “abandoning” his team with the sale of Marc Guéhi to Manchester City, the Austrian left this summer for Nottingham Forest, where he became the fifth head coach in less than a year. You can probably guess where this is going. After Saturday’s defeat by Brentford left Forest 19th, Glasner was typically abrasive: “If the players buy in [to my methods], I think we can have a turnaround. And if not, the sixth manager will come in.” We’ll soon find out if Glasner has won the players around with City visiting Nottingham this weekend. If Forest’s woeful form continues, Glasner may either walk or get his marching orders from trigger-happy owner Evangelos Marinakis. Michael Butler

Nottingham Forest v Manchester City, 5.30pm

Pierre Sage pulled no punches in his assessment of Eddie Nketiah’s career after his excellent winning goal against Fulham last week in the EFL Cup. “I think his potential is higher than his performance for the moment,” said the Crystal Palace manager. “And we have to reduce this gap – because he needs it, the team needs it.” Nketiah is now 27 and won his solitary senior cap for England in 2023 after becoming the record scorer for the under-21s while he was still at Arsenal. His chances of adding to that look unlikely these days having been relegated to third-choice striker at Selhurst Park behind Jean-Philippe Mateta and Jørgen Strand Larsen. But with Mateta suspended for three matches after Palace’s appeal over his red card against Tottenham was rejected, Sunday’s meeting with Liverpool could be another chance for Nketiah to show his ability after scoring a late winner against them in Palace’s thrilling win over Arne Slot’s side last year. EA

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

It has been three weeks since Brighton’s fans were savouring a famous 5-0 thrashing of arch-rivals Crystal Palace at the Amex. But the quirks of the fixture list meant that Fabian Hürzeler’s side played five successive games on the road since then, including two trips to face Manchester City. While they lost some of the momentum from the Palace victory in the defeat by Liverpool at Anfield, last week’s Premier League win over City showed they are serious contenders for the top four and perhaps even more. But Brentford’s visit to the south coast should provide a real test of those credentials as the owner, Tony Bloom, renews his rivalry with his west London counterpart Matthew Benham, who traditionally eschews his seat in the directors’ box for one in the away section. EA

Eli Junior Kroupi did not take the headlines in Bournemouth’s win over Leeds but his cheeky nutmeg and sumptuous no-look pass to set up Amine Adli for the Cherries’ second goal was a reminder of why the youngster continues to be linked with a move to Arsenal, Bayern and PSG, despite being sidelined recently. Kroupi scored 13 Premier League goals last season – only Ryan Giggs, Wayne Rooney, Michael Owen and Romelu Lukaku have reached that total as teenagers – and only Erling Haaland and Will Osula enjoyed a better goal-per-game ratio in 2025-26. Kroupi’s foot injury, sustained in pre-season, has been carefully managed but after three substitute appearances in the wins over Manchester United, Aston Villa and Leeds, will Marco Rose be tempted to start Kroupi at Ipswich? Evanilson is in red hot form as the central striker but Kroupi is just as comfortable as a No 10 and surely has to play if he is fit as Bournemouth chase a seventh consecutive win in all competitions. MB

It is not quite six months since Sunderland beat Chelsea 2-1 at the Stadium of Light and qualified for Europe for the first time in 53 years. A day when Régis Le Bris’s side eclipsed the Club World Cup holders and Enzo Le Fée upstaged Enzo Fernández concluded with Sunderland finishing seventh. During the prolonged post-match celebrations, the captain, Granit Xhaka, grabbed a microphone and declared: “This is just the beginning – we want more.” Fast forward to gloomy November and it is not quite working out like that with even the excitement of next month’s trip to Milan overshadowed by anxiety. Sunderland are bottom of the Premier League, Le Bris concedes his side are in a relegation fight and Xhaka has looked a shadow of his former self ever since Chelsea tried and failed to sign him in the summer. Is Xhaka capable of turning back time? Sunderland securing just a second league win of the season depends on it. Louise Taylor

Can Manchester United win two games of football on the bounce for the first time this season? Being able to pose this question sums up the state Michael Carrick and his side are in as they seek to build on Tuesday’s chaotic comeback victory over Roma. Aston Villa arrive five points better off than their hosts and are everything United are not: stable, upwardly mobile and boasting a manager in Unai Emery who consistently improves players and yields results. As the international break beckons after this last Premier League fixture of the weekend, will Carrick survive what could be an eighth loss in 15 matches? Possibly, if it is not another humiliation, but the truth may be that even United’s erratic executives do not currently know the answer. Jamie Jackson

Manchester United v Aston Villa, Sunday 4.30pm

Fantasy Football 2026: Week 9 preview — Start/sit and more

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Welcome to Week 9. Somehow, we are already at the halfway point of the 18-week NFL season. That was fast.

Week 8 was strange. Tampa beat Atlanta 16-0 without completing a single pass. Dallas and Kansas City both blew double-digit fourth quarter leads. Joe Burrow accounted for six total touchdowns. Running backs absolutely ran wild on Sunday. Baltimore lost two quarterbacks to what might be season-ending injuries in the first half at Buffalo. Minnesota held Detroit to its lowest point total of the season, but also scored the fewest against them, and absorbed its first loss. The Giants grew their NFC East lead without playing. It was that kind of weekend – eerie and weird — kind of fitting for Halloween.

What's in store this week? Six teams – Philadelphia, Seattle, Baltimore, Minnesota, fCleveland, and Arizona – are switching to a different starting quarterback. That raises a bunch of questions and decisions for fantasy managers.

Stats of the Week, Bucs-Falcons (sorry, this HAS TO get its own section – one day you'll tell your grandkids about that time in 2026 when two NFL teams played a high school football game):

Tampa became the first team since 1974, and the third since the forward pass was invented in the 1930s, to win a game without completing a single pass.

Tampa is the first team in NFL history to have two players (Bucky Irving and Kenneth Gainwell) with 25+ rushes and 125+ rushing yards in the same game.

Tampa's defenders caught four more passes than Tampa's offensive players.

Despite not completing a pass, and rushing for just 14 yards with no TDs, Baker Mayfield had more fantasy points than, and triple the passer rating of, Michael Penix, Jr.

Over their past two games (both wins, with 59 total points scored), the Bucs called 16 passes and 104 runs, and Baker Mayfield had seven completions for 64 yards.

No team in AFC North is over .500. Every team in the NFC North is over .500. Every team in the NFC South is under .500.

The NFC North is a combined 22-3 in non-divisional games.

Bo Nix has engineered 13 fourth quarter comebacks – the most in NFL history for a quarterback in his first three seasons.

Since the start of last season, the Broncos are 3-3 in games where they trailed by 11+ points during the fourth quarter. The rest of the NFL is 11-215 in such situations.

For the first time since 2003, six running backs had at least 140 rushing yards on Sunday.

The Browns have lost 23 straight games in Pittsburgh, which ties the NFL record for a road losing streak vs. one team.

The Bears have 17 rushing touchdowns, which is the most for a team through their first eight games since 1956.

Since 2000, Arizona was 1-147-1 when trailing by 14 points or more in the fourth quarter, and Dallas was 69-0 at home when leading by 14 points or more in the fourth quarter. That all changed on Sunday with the Cardinals' late comeback.

On to Week 9. Note: CIN @ ATL is being played in Spain. Set those lineups early!

For those who are familiar with this column, you know the drill. For everyone else: The Rides, Fades, and Sleepers is an analysis of players that I think are primed for an especially strong or poor performance, often as compared to consensus expectations. This isn't a straight-up Start/Sit exercise. As a general rule, start your studs. I'll rarely list the most obvious names at a position as "Rides" because those players are almost always expected to have strong performances. You don't need me or anyone else telling you to start Joe Burrow. While this analysis is intended for season-long play, it works for DFS formats too. Half PPR scoring and Expert Consensus Rankings (ECR) are used for the column.

Each week, I'm picking a Ride, Fade, and Sleeper of the Week. The rules: The Ride of the Week can't be a truly elite option, the Fade can't be someone who nobody is starting anyway, and the Sleeper must be an actual sleeper, from down in the rankings.

Last week, my Rides, Fades, and Sleepers did well, with a lot more hits than misses. You can check my work here.

My biggest miss was my Ride of the Week, Zay Flowers. He scored zero points. I'll repeat that – A bagel. A donut. Zero point zero. I obviously couldn't foresee the quarterback injuries, but I'll take the L. My Fade of the Week hit (Bryce Young, 11.4 fantasy points, QB18) and so did my Sleeper of the Week (Geno Smith, 19 fantasy points, QB13).

Ride of the Week: Matthew Stafford (@WAS). Stafford is ranked outside the Top-10 QBs this week and I think that's too low. The Commanders can score behind Jayden Daniels and they also give up points. This game's Vegas total of 50.5 is the second highest on the board and I think it goes over. Washington is tough to run on, but generous in the passing game. They're one of seven teams that has allowed at least 15 TD passes so far this season, and they've given up the third most fantasy points per game (FPPG) to opposing QBs. Stafford is coming off his second worst fantasy output of the season and I think he'll rebound. He came out of last week's game with a sore elbow, so keep an eye on that.

Fade of the Week: Zay Flowers (vs. JAC). Well, this is a first for me, as Flowers goes from Ride of the Week to Fade of the Week in just five days. UDFA rookie Joe Fagnano gets his first NFL start for the Ravens, on a short week and a bad ankle. If the Ravens get to ten points, I'll be surprised. Their implied total is 15, which is the second lowest on the Board, and they haven't topped 13 points in any of their last three games. Jacoby Brissett will help – in Week 10. Jacksonville's defense must be licking its chops after it throttled another dead team last week. Flowers is a terrific receiver who was having a great season before last Sunday, but this QB situation is too much to overcome. He's ranked inside the Top-20 at the position this week and I have him outside my Top-30.

Sleeper of the Week: Drew Lock (vs. ARI). The Cardinals are coming off that improbable comeback in Dallas, while Seattle will look to rebound after getting spanked by the Bears at home. This should be a get-right game for the Seahawks, and a potential let-down affair for the Cardinals (who had a let-down two weeks ago, after their big upset win over the Rams in Week 6). Seattle may struggle to run the ball against a defense that just stymied Javonte Williams. Arizona has allowed the most TD passes (18) and second most FPPG to opposing QBs. Lock is ranked as the QB29 this week, which is absurd. He's behind Fagnano, Gardner Minshew II, and Shedeur Sanders? No. None of them have JSN to throw to. Obviously, he shouldn't be started over a Top-12 option, but I like him as a QB2 this week. In his only start of the season in Week 2, he put up 21 fantasy points at…you guessed it…Arizona.

Elite options this week – Josh Allen, Lamar, Drake Maye, Brock Purdy, Jared Goff, and Joe Burrow;the analysis starts below them.

Three QBs ranked in the Top-10 this week who should smash:

Dak Prescott (@IND). The Colts have given up, or at least, they don't have the ability to compete at the moment. Dak should have his way with this mediocre defense, which has allowed the tenth most FPPG to opposing QBs. Dallas can't afford another stupid loss.

Trevor Lawrence (@BAL). The Ravens have a decent defense that's middle of the pack against opposing QBs, but there's almost no scenario where they don't lose this game by a lot. Their offense won't be able to stay on the field, so Lawrence should have plenty of scoring chances.

Patrick Mahomes (vs. NYJ). Mahomes threw for 321 yards and three TDs at Denver, to break a mini-cold streak and this week he gets a much softer defense, at home. Kenneth Walker might run wild, but I think Mahomes will get his too. The Jets have allowed 14 TD passes this season and have only one pick (ahem, that's since the start of last season).

Malik Willis (vs. DET) is in play this week as a low-end QB1 streamer. He's thrown for two TDs in two straight games and added a rushing TD in Week 7. The Lions' defense is showing improvement over the last two weeks, but they're still vulnerable and while they got their safeties back, now they're without stalwart LB Jack Campbell. They've allowed the most passing yards, passing TDs (18) and FPPG to opposing QBs, and they've also yielded two rushing TDs to the position.

Other QB2-types to consider if you need it: C.J. Stroud (@LAC), Kirk Cousins (@SF), Bo Nix (@CAR), and J.J. McCarthy (@PHI). Carson Wentz (@BUF) is risky, but it's an excellent matchup.

There are some higher-end starters with less-than-ideal matchups, but none are bad enough for me to list them as Fades. However, there are some QB2s who fantasy managers could be considering this week, but that I'm avoiding if I can: Baker Mayfield (@CHI, and I'm not sure how much he can really throw right now), and Michael Penix, Jr. (vs. CIN, and if you started him last week, I'm sorry for your loss).

And don't start Justin Herbert (vs. HOU). This is now a running joke.

Elite options this week – Jahmyr Gibbs, Bijan Robinson, Kenneth Walker, and Christian McCaffrey; the analysis starts below them.

Three high-end backs in smash spots this week:

Javonte Williams (@IND) barely averaged two yards per carry last week, a week after setting a career high in rushing yards. Which Williams will we get this week? A good one, I expect. Indianapolis's run defense isn't as stout without DeForest Buckner and allows the tenth most FPPG to opposing RBs. The Colts are reeling and offered very little resistance to Jacksonville's ground game last week (the first without Buckner). Williams is among the league leaders in carries inside the five and should bounce back nicely in this spot.

Bucky Irving (@CHI) and Bhayshul Tuten (@BAL) are coming off monster games and should rack up some good totals again this week. Chicago is tougher up the gut with Jeffery Simmons in the middle, but Tampa will run it and then run it some more. The Ravens are a tough run defense, but game script is a big factor here. Jacksonville is a 10-point favorite and should dominate time of possession and play with a big lead. If Tuten is out, Chris Rodriguez, Jr. is an OK play if you need him.

Others ranked inside the Top-25 this week who I think will outperform their rankings: Chase Brown (@ATL, who now allow the third most FPPG to opposing RBs after getting run over for three straight hours on Sunday), Chuba Hubbard (vs. DEN), Jonah Coleman (@CAR, allowing the second most FPPG to opposing RBs), and Quinshon Judkins (@NO, most FPPG allowed to opposing RBs). If Chase Brown can't get the ground game going this week, I'll start to get worried.

A few to consider if you're stuck: Woody Marks (@LAC), Alvin Kamara (vs. CLE), Mike Washington, Jr. (@SF), Dylan Sampson (vs. CLE), and J.K. Dobbins (@CAR).

I've got some household names on the list this week.

Derrick Henry (vs. JAC) is a tough call this week. The Ravens are in a terrible situation for this game, on a short week. Jacksonville has been a buzzsaw for running backs. They allow the fewest yards and FPPG to opposing RBs. Henry is outside my Top-15 for the week. Henry scored last week, but his fantasy output has taken a huge hit since Lamar Jackson got hurt.

Is Jonathan Taylor (vs. DAL) really going to be active on Sunday? It's a tasty matchup (see, e.g., Allgeier, Tyler), but if he plays he could see limited snaps while potential buyers consider their offers and the Colts try to protect the asset. Start him if he's active, but with caution.

It's tough to start Saquon Barkley (@NYG), who is dealing with multiple injuries and isn't a lock to get a decent workload. Yes, he has two straight games with a TD, but in each of those games he played fewer than 20 snaps. I'm sure he still gets a little extra motivation against his old team, but they've been pretty good against running backs (Top-12 fantasy run defense).

Josh Jacobs (@NE) struggled to find running room last week and looked slow. He got bailed out with two short touchdowns, and that's his upside. Green Bay hasn't run the ball well all season, and New England allows the fifth fewest FPPG to opposing RBs. Pass.

The Seahawks struggled to run the ball consistently against Chicago, and it won't be all that much easier against Arizona, who is decent vs. the run. Throw in that Seattle is using multiple backs in rotation and I'll fade Zach Charbonnet and Jadarian Price (vs. ARI).

PSA: Don't start any Chargers RBs. Of all players who don't have injury as an excuse, Omarion Hampton is currently the single most disappointing player who went in the first two rounds of fantasy drafts.

Other RB Fades: Cam Skattebo (@PHI, if he plays), Ashton Jeanty (@SF), and Blake Corum (@WAS).

Elite options this week – Puka Nacua, Jaxon Smith-Njigba, Ja'Marr Chase AND Tee Higgins, Amon-Ra St. Brown, CeeDee Lamb, and Malik Nabers; the analysis starts below them.

I've listed seven "elites" above. Beyond them, here are six Rides this week, all ranked inside the weekly Top-20: Christian Watson AND Matthew Golden (@NE, tough matchup but they can't run the ball and will have to throw a lot), Parker Washington (to bounce back, @BAL), Rashee Rice (vs. NYJ), Justin Jefferson (vs. BUF, they've allowed the most TD catches and fifth most FPPG to opposing WRs, and the Vikings will actually need to score to win this game), and Luther Burden III (vs. TB).

10 Greatest American Martial Arts Stars Of All Time, Ranked

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The martial arts movie genre has changed a lot over the years, and many classics still hold up today. Those older films can feel like time capsules, and fans can find something to appreciate in them even if they lack the stylish, snappy choreography of their more modern counterparts.

Martial arts go hand in hand with the action genre, and audiences of all ages can appreciate stylish brawls that are far more intense and personal than any gunfight. Naturally, the people throwing those punches and kicks are the stars of the show, as making those fights believable is a physical and technical feat.

Few actors in the genre are as famous as Jackie Chan. He's a pioneer in modern martial arts cinema, adding humor and intricate stunt choreography to his films. It's a unique combination, especially compared to other classic fighting movies that are notably more grounded and violent.

Donnie Yen is another legendary actor in the space. He's a phenomenal martial artist and also choreographed many of his own fight scenes. Yen doesn't just know how to throw punches and kicks; he makes them look impactful. He's also starred in numerous classic martial arts movies, including the Ip Man movies.

Several notable American martial artists have also left their mark on martial arts cinema. The actors on this list are all beloved for their roles in a range of movies. They also have genuine martial arts training, and some still practice today.

Mark Dacascos has an expansive filmography with a career spanning decades. Many action movie fans will recognize him as Zero in John Wick: Chapter 3 - Parabellum, but he was also particularly prominent during the 90s and early 2000s.

Dacascos starred as Louis Stevens in Only the Strong in 1993. He's a former special forces officer and attempts to teach troubled students' capoeira. It's a remarkable movie because it was so unusual to see the fighting style in the spotlight at the time. He also starred in Drive and Double Dragon.

Martial arts are especially important to Dacascos because his father, Al Dacascos, founded Wun Hop Kuen Do. He's a lifelong martial artist who has reportedly trained in Muay Thai, Capoeira, and Wushu alongside his father's style.

Jeff Speakman boasts an extensive martial arts background and teaches Kenpo 5.0, which he founded. Speakman also reportedly has a black belt in Goju-Ryu Karate and a 10th-degree Kenpo black belt.

His filmography is less expansive than some other martial artists on this list, though that doesn't take away from his accomplishments in the genre. The Perfect Weapon is arguably his best movie and his breakout role. Speakman stars as Jeff Sanders in a one-man-army revenge story, serving as the ideal platform to showcase his Kenpo skills.

Fans of classic martial arts movies will enjoy The Perfect Weapon because Speakman's style is unique and full of stylish combinations. He also starred in several other 90s movies, including Street Knight and Deadly Outbreak.

Fans of martial arts movies will likely know Jim Kelly best as Williams in Enter the Dragon. His fights in the tournament and with Han are highlights of the movie, and he was a prominent actor in the genre in the 70s.

Away from the silver screen, Kelly won the middleweight title at the Long Beach International Karate Championships in 1971. He's stylish and agile, and his fights look authentic because he really does know how to throw punches and kicks.

Kelly also starred in several movies after Enter the Dragon, including Black Belt Jones in 1974. He also reprised the role in Hot Potato.

Philip Rhee is best known as Tommy Lee in the martial arts drama, Best of the Best. The US Taekwondo team is set to compete against the Korean team. They are the underdogs, but Tommy Lee emerges as their star fighter despite his history with Dae Han (Simon Rhee), a Korean fighter. The original Best of the Best is arguably one of the best American martial arts movies of the 20th century, and James Earl Jones even plays the US coach.

Rhee co-wrote Best of the Best, produced Best of the Best II, and made his directorial debut with Best of the Best 3: No Turning Back. He also starred as Tommy Lee in each movie.

Interestingly, Rhee is also a proven martial artist outside the movies. He began studying at an early age and holds black belts in multiple disciplines, including Taekwondo, Hapkido, and Kendo.

Cynthia Rothrock has an extraordinary martial arts record. She was a five-time world champion in forms and weapons between 1981 and 1985. She also holds multiple black belts in styles including Taekwondo and Tang Soo Do.

That extensive training translates beautifully onto the silver screen. Yes, Madam! is arguably one of her most important movies, starring Rothrock alongside Michelle Yeoh. Rothrock also led China O'Brien, a martial arts action movie where she plays a police officer forced to rely on her skills when she moves back to her quiet town in Utah.

Cynthia Rothrock is so influential in the genre that some fans call her the Queen of Martial Arts Movies. She has appeared in dozens of films, including more recent projects such as 2024's The Last Kumite.

Wesley Snipes is a beloved action star, best known for his portrayal of Blade, who's part human, part vampire. In the original Blade movie, he attempts to stop a vampire called Deacon Frost (Stephen Dorff) from completing an ancient ritual.

Blade is a flashy, stylish fighter, and quite convincing, as Snipes has martial arts training. In fact, he reportedly started at 12 years old and studied Goju-Ryu and Shotokan Karate. Snipes has also allegedly studied other styles, including Capoeira.

While Wesley Snipes certainly has real-world martial arts experience, his films are more action-focused than strictly fighting movies. One exception is Undisputed, which is more of a prison drama, but Snipes looks so authentic in his fight scenes thanks to his training.

Don 'The Dragon' Wilson is widely considered a kickboxing legend. He's an 11-time world kickboxing champion with multiple Hall of Fame entries for his achievements in the ring. His nickname isn't unfounded either, as he studied Pai Lum White Dragon Kung Fu.

Wilson is a kickboxing icon, and while he's less known among casual moviegoers, many martial arts fans will know him from the long-running Bloodfist franchise. He also starred in Ring of Fire as Johnny Woo, which started a trilogy.

Wilson's competition fighting experience translates well into his movie action scenes. He remains a respected name in kickboxing, even if his films are less well-known among general audiences.

Michael Jai White is arguably an underrated modern action star. He's starred in multiple martial arts movies, though Blood and Bone is a fan favorite. He stars as Isaiah Bone in the 2009 movie, an ex-convict who enters an underground fight circuit and quickly makes a name for himself.

The fights in Blood and Bone are stylish and brutal, and White has also earned impressive accolades outside the movies. He started training in martial arts at a young age and holds black belts in several Karate styles. White even demonstrates some of his techniques on his official YouTube channel.

Michael Jai White's moves look incredible in his films. The choreography is exceptional, and brawls feel intense and grounded.

Chuck Norris became an action movie star and a household name throughout his career. He has well-documented martial arts roots and a proven track record in contact sports.

Norris was also an important part of pop culture, with memes and even books telling grandiose tales about his strength. His real-world performances were just as impressive, as he held the World Professional Middleweight Karate Championship title for six consecutive years. Norris also trained alongside Bruce Lee and played Colt in The Way of the Dragon.

Chuck Norris has an expansive filmography and was one of the biggest action stars of the 80s. He also founded a martial arts system called Chun Kuk Do.

Bruce Lee was born in California in 1940 and is widely regarded as one of the most influential martial arts movie stars of all time. His contribution to the genre cannot be overstated, and his influence on fight choreography and action cinema can still be felt decades later. He also founded his own combat system called Jeet Kune Do.

Lee starred in several movies, though Enter the Dragon is his most popular. He enters a tournament on Mr. Han's (Shih Kien) island as a competitor. However, he's also there to gather evidence, as British authorities suspect Han is running a drug operation there.

Bruce Lee's fight scenes are incredible. His speed is utterly jaw-dropping, particularly during his encounter with Han's bodyguard, Oharra (Robert Wall). His bout with Chuck Norris in The Way of the Dragon is another classic scene and is among the most famous martial arts movie showdowns of all time.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of ScreenRant, directly in your inbox.
